\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma}

with all coefficients in
L
. Here
\alpha
is the inverse of the final isomorphism in (
2.4.6
), and
\beta,\beta^{\prime}
are the compositions of the forgetful maps
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{\operatorname{an}}\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{L[G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})]}
with the identification (
2.4.3
) (and its analogue for
\operatorname{I}_{\overline{P}_{2}}^{\operatorname{sm}}
). The proposition then follows if we can show

\Delta^{\operatorname{la}}(\lambda)=\beta^{\prime}\circ\alpha(\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}).

By injectivity of
\gamma
, it suffices to show that

\gamma(\Delta^{\operatorname{la}}(\lambda))=\beta(\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}).

As in Remark
2.4.2
, we see
\gamma(\Delta^{\operatorname{la}}(\lambda))
is represented by the cocycle

(2.4.8)

x\mapsto\Big(F\longmapsto\Big[g\mapsto\Big((\lambda\circ v_{1}\circ s)(gx)-(\lambda\circ v_{1}\circ s)(g)\Big)F(g)\Big]\Big),

for
F\in\operatorname{I}_{\overline{P}_{2}}^{\operatorname{sm}}(L)
. To compute
\beta(\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}})
, note that exactly as in Lemma
2.2.1
– and because of our choice of
\Lambda_{\lambda}
– we have an
L
-linear splitting of the extension
\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}
induced by

r\colon F\mapsto\left(g\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}(\lambda\circ v_{1}\circ s)(g)F(g)\\
F(g)\end{pmatrix}\right)\;\in\;\operatorname{I}_{\overline{B}}^{\operatorname{la}}(\sigma_{\Lambda_{\lambda}}),\qquad F\in\operatorname{I}_{\overline{P}_{2}}^{\operatorname{sm}}(L).

The associated cocycle representing
\beta(\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}})
is given by
x\mapsto(F\mapsto[x\cdot r(x^{-1}\cdot F)-r(F)])
, i.e.,

x\mapsto\left(F\longmapsto\left[g\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}\big((\lambda\circ v_{1}\circ s)(gx)-(\lambda\circ v_{1}\circ s)(g)\big)F(g)\\
0\end{pmatrix}\right]\right),

which agrees exactly with (
2.4.8
). Thus
\gamma(\Delta^{\operatorname{la}}(\lambda))=\beta(\mathcal{C}_{\Lambda_{\lambda}})
, as required.
∎

3.
p
-arithmetic cohomology

In this section, we set-up notation and recap
p
-arithmetic cohomology from
[
Geh21
]
. We also describe Eisenstein classes in this setting.

3.1.
Notation

Fix henceforth a neat compact open subgroup
K^{p}\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Recall
H=\GL_{2}\times\GL_{1}
, and let
K^{p}_{H}=K^{p}\cap H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
. Possibly shrinking
K^{p}
we assume
K^{p}_{H}\subset\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
.

For
G
, we will always work with Iwahori-level
\operatorname{Iw}\subset G(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
at
p
; but it will be necessary to vary the level at
p
for
H
. For
U\subset H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
any open compact subgroup, consider the finite set:

A_{U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\backslash H(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{p}_{H}U,

and let
I_{U}\subset H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a set of representatives for
A_{U}
. For any
x\in I_{U}
, we denote by

\Gamma_{U}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap xK^{p}_{H}Ux^{-1}

the corresponding arithmetic subgroup. As we assumed
K^{p}
neat, the group
\Gamma_{U}(x)
is torsion-free.

3.2.
Representations and
p
-arithmetic cohomology

We recall notions from
[
Geh21
, §3]
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

Let
\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))
denote the set of connected components of
H(\mathbb{R})
.

•

For an
R[H(\mathbb{Q})]
-module
M
, set

\mathcal{A}_{H}(M)=\left\{f\colon\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))\times H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{p}_{H}\to M\right\}

This is a left-
H(\mathbb{Q})
-module via

(\gamma\cdot f)([h_{\infty}],h^{p})=\gamma\cdot f([\gamma^{-1}h_{\infty}],\gamma^{-1}h^{p}),\qquad[h_{\infty}]\in\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R})),\ h^{p}\in H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K_{H}^{p}.

•

Let
D_{H}
be the Steinberg module of
H
, as described in
[
Geh21
, Def. 3.21]
, and set
\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(M)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb{Z}}(D_{H},\mathcal{A}_{H}(M))
. If
f\in\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(M)
we write
f(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f(d)([h_{\infty}],h^{p})
, where
d\in D_{H}
. We obtain a left
H(\mathbb{Q})
-action by

(\gamma\cdot f)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p})=\gamma\cdot f(\gamma^{-1}\cdot d,[\gamma^{-1}h_{\infty}],\gamma^{-1}h^{p}).

•

For
M
an
R[G(\mathbb{Q})]
-module, define
G(\mathbb{Q})
-modules
\mathcal{A}_{G}(M)
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(M)
by direct analogy.

A particularly important case is where
M
is a space of functions on a set
X
; for example,
M=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)^{*}
or
M=\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R
. We can then naturally represent elements of
\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(M)
as functions

D_{H}\times\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))\times H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})/K^{p}_{H}\times X\to R.

Remark 3.2.2
.

We note some slight differences to
[
Geh21
]
. Because
H
and
G
are not semisimple, we include
\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))
and
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R}))
to account for connected components.

We also dualise: the notation
\mathcal{A}(K^{p}_{H},M;R)

op.cit.
corresponds to what we call
\mathcal{A}_{H}(M^{*})
, where
M^{*}
is the
R
-linear dual. Note in particular that via the duality between compact induction and regular induction, Gehrmann’s space
\mathcal{A}(K^{p}_{H}\times U;R)=\mathcal{A}(K^{p}_{H},\operatorname{c-Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R;R)
is, in our notation, denoted
\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)
(and similarly for
\mathcal{A}_{H,c}
,
\mathcal{A}_{G}
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}
).

Remark 3.2.3
.

The cohomology of the above representations (“
p
-arithmetic cohomology”) provides a reinterpretation of classical arithmetic cohomology. To see this, recall
U\subset H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
is an open compact subgroup, with attached objects
I_{U}
and
\Gamma_{U}(x)
as in §
3.4
. If
R
is a ring, then Shapiro’s lemma defines a natural isomorphism

(3.2.4)

\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\bigoplus_{x\in I_{U}}\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(\Gamma_{U}(x),R\right).

We also have a compactly-supported version

(3.2.5)

\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)\xrightarrow{\,\smash{\raisebox{-1.959pt}{$\scriptstyle\sim$}}\,}\bigoplus_{x\in I_{U}}\operatorname{H}^{i}\Big(\Gamma_{U}(x),\operatorname{Hom}(D_{H},R)\Big),

where
\Gamma_{U}(x)
acts on
\operatorname{Hom}(D_{H},R)
by
(y\cdot f)(d)=f(y^{-1}d)
. Explicitly, (
3.2.5
) takes a cocycle
c
to the collection of cocycles
(c_{x})_{x\in I_{U}}
satisfying

c_{x}[y_{1},\dots,y_{i}](d)=c[y_{1},\dots,y_{i}](d,[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x^{p},x_{p})

where
d\in D_{H}
and
y_{j}\in\Gamma_{U}(x)
.

Gehrmann uses the above, together with the familiar identity between arithmetic and Betti cohomology, to show the following. Let
Z_{G}
be the centre of
G
, let
K_{\infty}=Z_{G}(\mathbb{R})O_{3}(\mathbb{R})
, let
K_{\infty}^{H}=K_{\infty}\cap H(\mathbb{R})
, and let
K_{\infty}^{\circ}
and
(K_{\infty}^{H})^{\circ}
be their neutral components. Then let

X_{G,K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=G(\mathbb{Q})\backslash G(\mathbb{A})/K_{\infty}^{\circ}K^{p}\operatorname{Iw},\qquad X_{H,K^{p}_{H}U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=H(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{A})/(K_{\infty}^{H})^{\circ}K^{p}_{H}U.

Proposition 3.2.6
.

(i)

For all
i\geqslant 0
, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{i}\left(X_{G,K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},R\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{i}\left(X_{H,K^{p}_{H}U},R\right).

(ii)

For all
i\geqslant 0
, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{i+2}_{\mathrm{c}}\left(X_{G,K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},R\right),

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{i}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{H}^{i+1}_{\mathrm{c}}\left(X_{H,K^{p}_{H}U},R\right).

Proof.

Part (i) is proved after
[
Geh21
, Cor. 3.4]
. Part (ii) is explained in §3.6
op. cit
. We recall that there
l
is used to denote the number of simple roots, so
l=2
for
G
and
l=1
for
H
, explaining the degree shift in (ii). The only difference from
op. cit
. is that we must include
\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R}))
to handle connected components, but given this all of the relevant isomorphisms and arguments pass over
mutatis mutandis
to the reductive setting.
∎

Now, there is a natural pairing

\langle-,-\rangle_{U}\colon\operatorname{H}^{i-1}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)\times\operatorname{H}^{2-i}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)\to R

corresponding to Poincaré duality on the Betti cohomology. It is equivariant for the
H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-action (up to necessary modifications to
K^{p}_{H}
), and restriction and corestriction are adjoint under the pairing. Note that the pairing is not perfect in general (but we will not need it to be).

3.3.
p
-arithmetic cohomology for the Steinberg representation

We consider another important
p
-arithmetic cohomology group. The following is a variant on
[
Geh21
, Prop. 3.6(b)]
.

Let
\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}:\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to R
denote the characteristic function
\operatorname{ch}(\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cdot\operatorname{Iw})
. When
R=L
, a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, or
R=\mathcal{O}_{L}
, its ring of integers, the image of
\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}
in
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)
generates the Iwahori-invariants
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)^{\operatorname{Iw}}
. We then have a natural
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant map

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)^{*}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R,

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\longmapsto\big(g\mapsto\nu(g\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})\big).

This induces a map

\rho_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i}\colon\mathrm{H}^{i}\Big(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)^{*})\Big)\to\mathrm{H}^{i}\Big(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{Ind}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{\operatorname{Iw}}R)\Big)\cong\mathrm{H}^{i+2}_{\mathrm{c}}\Big(X_{G,K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},R\Big).

Consider the Hecke operator
U_{p,1}=[\operatorname{Iw}t\operatorname{Iw}]
acting on
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)^{\operatorname{Iw}}
, recalling
t=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.

Lemma 3.3.1
.

The operator
U_{p,1}
acts trivially on the image of
\rho_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i}
.

Proof.

It is standard that
\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}
is fixed by
U_{p,1}
. Write
U_{p,1}=\sum_{j}\gamma_{j}
; then
U_{p,1}\cdot\rho_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i}
is the map induced by

\displaystyle\mu\mapsto\Big(g\mapsto\sum_{j}\mu(g\gamma_{j}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(g\mapsto\mu\Big(g\cdot\Big[\sum_{j}\gamma_{j}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}\Big]\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(g\mapsto\mu(g\cdot U_{p,1}\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})\Big)=\Big(g\mapsto\mu(g\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})\Big),

which also induces
\rho_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{i}
.
∎

Remark 3.3.2
.

In
[
Geh21
, Prop. 3.6]
, Gehrmann proves that
\rho_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{i}
is an isomorphism after passing to
\pi
-isotypic components, where
\pi
is any automorphic representation that is Steinberg at
p
; see Proposition
6.1.2
of the present paper.

The group
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R))
can be considered as a space of ‘modular symbols’ for
\GL_{3}
. The space
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(R)^{*}))
is then an analogue of modular symbols over the Bruhat–Tits tree, and Gehrmann’s result says any Steinberg-at-
p
eigensymbol can be lifted to the tree. For explicit analogues in the case of Bianchi modular forms, see
[
BW19
, §4]
or
[
VW21
, §3.3]
.

3.4.
Level subgroups at
p

We now introduce some level subgroups at
p
. Let
n\geqslant 1
, and define

{{{{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 5.75555pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{h_{p}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\times 1\ \text{modulo }p^{n}\right\}}$}}}}{\hbox{\kern-110.21982pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{h_p \equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1 &\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right) \times 1 \ \text{modulo }p^n}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-18.38382pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U(p^{n})^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.77779pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cap}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern-8.02084pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=}$}}}}}}}\right\}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.623pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{h_{p}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\times x\ \text{modulo }p^{n}\right\}}$}}}{\hbox{\kern-55.74184pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{h_p \equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x &\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right) \times x \ \text{modulo }p^n}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-18.9602pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.33334pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.77779pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\cap}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\kern-8.02084pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=}$}}}}}}}\right\}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.67355pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{h_{p}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\times*\ \text{modulo }p^{n}\right\}}$}}}{\hbox{\kern-55.74184pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{h_p \equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}* &\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right) \times* \ \text{modulo }p^n}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-18.32826pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U_{1}(p^{n})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-8.02084pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=}$}}}}}}}\right\}}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.67355pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\left\{h_{p}\equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}*&*\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\times*\ \text{modulo }p^{n}\right\}}$}}}{\hbox{\kern-55.74184pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{h_p \equiv\left(\begin{smallmatrix}* &*\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right) \times* \ \text{modulo }p^n}}{\hbox{\kern-6.8889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\subset}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-18.207pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H(\mathbb{Z}_{p}).}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces

Recalling
A_{U},I_{U}
and
\Gamma_{U}(x)
from §
3.1
, we adopt the notation

A_{1}(p^{n})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=A_{U_{1}(p^{n})},\qquad I_{1}(p^{n})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=I_{U_{1}(p^{n})},\qquad\Gamma_{1}(p^{n})_{x}=\Gamma_{U_{1}(p^{n})}(x),

and similarly for
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge},U(p^{n})^{\circ}
and
U(p^{n})
. Note that
A_{1}(p^{n})\cong\mathcal{A}_{1}\times\mathcal{A}_{2}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{i}
are finite groups which are quotients of groups of the form
\left(\mathbb{Z}/D_{i}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
with
p\nmid D_{i}
.

We can and do fix a choice
I_{1}(p^{n})=I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\subset H(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})=\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
, considered in
H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
by placing 1 at
p
.

Lemma 3.4.1
.

For
n\geqslant 1
we have
H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap H(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\subset U(p^{n})
. In particular, if
x\in I_{1}(p^{n})
, we have
\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}\subset U(p^{n})
.

Proof.

If
y\in H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap H(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
, write
y=(y_{1},y_{2})
with
y_{1}\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
and
y_{2}\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Then
\operatorname{det}y_{1}
and
y_{2}
are positive; and they are
\ell
-adic units for all primes
\ell
. Hence
\det y_{1}=y_{2}=1
. The
p
-component of
y_{1}
lies in
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
by definition, hence
y_{1}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})
has the form
y_{1}\equiv\operatorname{diag}(z,1)

\pmod{p^{n}}
. Since
\det(y_{1})=1
, one must have
z=1
(modulo
p^{n}
), hence
y_{1}
is congruent to the identity modulo
p^{n}
, and
y\in U(p^{n})
.

The final statement follows as
\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}=H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap xK^{p}_{H}U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}x^{-1}
, and
xK^{p}_{H}x^{-1}\subset H(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{p})
.
∎

3.5.
Eisenstein classes

Let
c
be a positive integer prime to
6pD_{2}
. Let
\eta_{2}
be an even Dirichlet character of conductor
D|D_{2}
, and we assume that
\eta_{2}
factors through
\left(\mathbb{Z}/D_{2}\mathbb{Z}\right)\twoheadrightarrow A_{2}
. We assume that the local component of
K^{p}_{H}
at any prime
\ell|c
is
H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
. Let
{{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})}
be the Schwartz functions which vanish at
(0,0)
and equal
\operatorname{ch}(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{2})
at any prime
\ell|c
(see
[
LW21
, §5.3.3]
). This carries a natural
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-action, and thus an
H(\mathbb{A}_{f})
-action via the natural projection
H\twoheadrightarrow\GL_{2}
.

For
\Phi\in{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})
fixed by
K^{p}_{H}U
with
U\subset H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
an open compact subgroup, let
{}_{c}\operatorname{Eis}_{\Phi}^{0}
be the associated Betti–Eisenstein class from
[
LW21
, Thm. 5.7]
; it is the Betti realisation of Beilinson’s motivic Eisenstein class from
[
Bei86
]
. Since we work in trivial weight, it admits a simpler description in terms of Siegel units (see, e.g.,
[
LW20
, §3.1]
). Via the
\GL_{2}
-analogue of Proposition
3.2.6
, we view
{}_{c}\operatorname{Eis}_{\Phi}^{0}\in\mathrm{H}^{1}(\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{\GL_{2}}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}}^{\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathbb{Z}))
, where
U_{\operatorname{GL}_{2}}
denotes the image of
U
under the natural map
H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{\eta_{2}}
denote the ring of integers of a number field in which
\eta_{2}
is valued. Then, we have a
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant association

{{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})}^{K^{p}_{H}U}\to\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathbb{Z}_{\eta_{2}})\right)

given by
\Phi\mapsto\mathcal{E}_{\Phi,U}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={{}_{c}\operatorname{Eis}_{\Phi}^{0}}\boxtimes[\widehat{\eta}_{2}]
, where
\boxtimes
is the exterior cup product (we are viewing
[\widehat{\eta}_{2}]
as a cohomology class for the
\operatorname{GL}_{1}
Shimura set). This map is
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant in that for any
g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}
), the following diagram commutes:

(3.5.1)

\textstyle{{{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})}^{K^{p}_{H}U}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g\cdot-}
\textstyle{\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathbb{Z}_{\eta_{2}})\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g\cdot-}
\textstyle{{{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})}^{K^{p}_{H}\cdot gUg^{-1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{gUg^{-1}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathbb{Z}_{\eta_{2}})\right).}

Here
g
acts on the right-hand spaces through its inclusion into
H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. In particular, if
U^{\prime}\subset U
and we can choose a set of representatives for
U/U^{\prime}
in
\operatorname{GL}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\times\{1\}\subset H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, then we have a trace map
\operatorname{tr}=\operatorname{tr}^{U}_{U^{\prime}}
and a compatibility

(3.5.2)

\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tr}(\Phi),U}=\mathrm{tr}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\Phi,U^{\prime}}\right)\in\mathrm{H}^{1}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathbb{Z}_{\eta_{2}})\right)\qquad\text{for all }\Phi\in{{}_{c}\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{2},\mathbb{Z})}^{K^{p}_{H}U^{\prime}}.

Notation 3.5.3
.

(i)

At
p
, we will have a particular interest in the Schwartz function
\Phi_{p}^{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{ch}(p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},1+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p})\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{2},\mathbb{Z})
, which we can naturally view with coefficients in
R
.

(ii)

We shall later need to make particular choices of prime-to-
p
Schwartz functions. When the choice of
\Phi^{(p)}
is fixed and implicit (for example, throughout §
4
), as far as possible we will drop it from notation. To this end, we will often write
\Phi_{n}
for the function
\Phi^{(p)}\Phi_{p}^{n}
.

4.
An abstract exceptional zero formula

Let
\mu\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))
. In this section, we attach a
p
-adic measure
\mathscr{L}(\mu)\in\mathcal{O}_{L}[\![\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}]\!]
to
\mu
, and then prove an abstract exceptional zero formula (with an abstract
\mathcal{L}
-invariant) for
\mathscr{L}(\mu)
.

Throughout,
p
is any prime,
L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}
is finite with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{L}
and uniformiser
\varpi
, such that
\eta_{2}
is valued in
\mathcal{O}_{L}^{\times}
. We continue to impose the assumptions in §
3.5
. To ease notation, we will drop the superscript “
\operatorname{sm}
” when discussing smooth representations. Unless otherwise specified,
R
will be a general
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra.

Convention
.

We will fix tame data
\Phi^{(p)}
and
K^{p}
, as defined in the previous section, and in this section drop them from all notation, adopting Notation
3.5.3
. We emphasise that all of our constructions will, however, depend on the choice of
\Phi^{(p)}
.

4.1.
Evaluation maps for
p
-arithmetic cohomology

We now define evaluation maps on the
p
-arithmetic cohomology that will allow us to construct the
p
-adic measure
\mathscr{L}_{\mu}
. First, we construct a
p
-arithmetic restriction map from
G
to
H
, for which we need the following data:

Notation 4.1.1
.

(i)

[
Ree90
, Prop. 1.1]
gives a
H(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariant injection
\iota\colon D_{H}\hookrightarrow D_{G}
.

(ii)

Recall we fixed, in §
3.4
, a set of representatives
I\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=I_{1}(p^{n})=I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\subset\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
.

(iii)

We have
\det(U(p^{n})^{\lozenge})=\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
; thus by strong approximation, for any
h_{\infty}\in H(\mathbb{R})
,
h^{p}\in H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
h_{p}\in H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
there exist
h\in H(\mathbb{Q})
,
x\in I
,
k^{p}\in K^{p}_{H}
and
k_{p}\in U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
such that

[h_{\infty}]=h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],\ h^{p}=hxk^{p},\ h_{p}=hk_{p}.

Here
x
is unique given
I
; but then
h,k^{p}
and
k_{p}
are only well-defined up to
\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}
, as for any
\gamma\in\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}
, we can write
[h_{\infty}]=h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}]=h\gamma[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}]
,
h^{p}=hxk^{p}=h\gamma\cdot x\cdot x^{-1}\gamma^{-1}xk^{p}
and
h_{p}=hk_{p}=h\gamma\cdot\gamma^{-1}k_{p}
.

Definition 4.1.2
.

Let
n\geqslant 1
. Define a map

\Psi_{n}:\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(R)^{*})\right)\times\operatorname{St}(R)^{U(p^{n})}\longrightarrow\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}\big(\operatorname{Ind}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}R\big)\right)

by

\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,\varphi),

for
h
and
x
as in (iii) above.

We must show this is well-defined. The only ambiguity in the definition comes through
h
, which, by (iii) above, we can replace with
h\gamma
for
\gamma\in\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}
.

Lemma 4.1.3
.

The function
\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)\in\mathcal{A}_{H,c}\left(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R\right)
is well-defined and independent of the choice of representatives
I
.

Proof.

First we consider well-definedness. We must check the definition is unchanged after replacing
h
with
h\gamma
for
\gamma\in\Gamma(p^{n})_{x}^{\lozenge}\subset H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
. By
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariance of
\mu
we have

(4.1.4)

\mu((h\gamma)^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,\varphi)=\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),\gamma[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],\gamma x,\gamma\cdot\varphi).

We have
\gamma\in U(p^{n})
by Lemma
3.4.1
, so
\gamma\varphi=\varphi
; and as
\gamma\in H(\mathbb{R})_{+}
we have
\gamma[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}]=[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}]
. Finally as
\gamma\in xK^{p}_{H}x^{-1}
we see
\gamma x=x\cdot x^{-1}\gamma x\in xK^{p}_{H}
. As
F
is right-invariant under
K^{p}_{H}
, we conclude the right-hand side of (
4.1.4
) is equal to
\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,\varphi)
. Thus
\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)
is a well-defined function
D_{H}\times\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))\times H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})\times H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to R
. It is right-invariant under
K^{p}_{H}
in the
h^{p}
-variable, since
\mu
is invariant under
K^{p}
; and it is right-invariant under
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
by construction. Thus
\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)\in\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)
is well-defined.

We now show it is independent of the choice of
I
. Let
I^{\prime}=\{x_{i}^{\prime}\}\subset\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
be another set of representatives, defining a function
\Psi_{n}^{\prime}(\mu,\varphi)\in\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)
. There exist
h_{i}\in H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}
,
k_{i}\in K^{p}_{H}
and
u_{i}\in U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
such that

x_{i}^{\prime}=h_{i}x_{i}k_{i}u_{i}\qquad\forall i.

By assumption
\mu
is invariant under
h_{i}
. As both
x_{i}^{\prime}
and
x_{i}
are trivial at
p
, we see
h_{i,p}=u_{i}^{-1}\in U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
. Moreover
h_{i}^{p}\in x_{i}^{\prime}K_{H}^{p}x_{i}^{-1}\subset\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
, so exactly as in Lemma
3.4.1
, we see
h_{i}\in U(p^{n})
. In particular
h_{i}^{-1}
fixes
\varphi
. Additionally
h_{i}^{-1}[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}]=[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}]
. We thus compute that

\displaystyle\Psi_{n}^{\prime}(\mu,\varphi)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{n}^{\prime}(\mu,\varphi)(d,h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],hx_{i}^{\prime}k^{p},hk_{p})=\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x_{i}^{\prime},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],h_{i}x_{i}k_{i},\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mu(h_{i}^{-1}h^{-1}\iota(d),h_{i}^{-1}[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x_{i},h_{i}^{-1}\varphi)

\displaystyle=\mu((hh_{i})^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x_{i},\varphi)=\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}).

The fourth equality is
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariance of
F
, and the last uses that
h^{p}=hx_{i}^{\prime}k^{p}=(hh_{i})x_{i}(k_{i}k^{p})
.
∎

Lemma 4.1.5
.

We have
\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)\in\mathrm{H}^{0}\Big(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\Big)
.

Proof.

We must show
\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)
is fixed by
H(\mathbb{Q})
. For
\gamma\in H(\mathbb{Q})
we have

\displaystyle(\gamma^{-1}\cdot\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi))(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)(\gamma d,\gamma[h_{\infty}],\gamma h^{p},\gamma h_{p})

\displaystyle=\mu((\gamma h)^{-1}\gamma d,[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,\varphi)=\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}),

where we write
h^{p}=hxk^{p}
as above, and note
(\gamma h)^{-1}\gamma=h^{-1}
.
∎

Remark 4.1.6
.

If
\varphi
is fixed by the larger group
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
, we have the simpler description

\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})=\mu(\iota(d),[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}\cdot\varphi).

The following is our
p
-arithmetic evaluation map. Recall
\Phi_{n}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\Phi,U}
from §
3.5
.

Definition 4.1.7
.

Define an
R
-bilinear map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}\colon\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(R)^{*})\right)\times\operatorname{St}(R)^{U(p^{n})}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow R

\displaystyle(\mu,\varphi)

\displaystyle\longmapsto\left\langle\Psi_{n}(\mu,\varphi),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}.

4.2.
p
-adic measures attached to
p
-arithmetic cohomology classes

The following definition and lemma, describing certain elements of and manipulations in
\operatorname{St}(R)^{*}
, will repeatedly be useful.

Definition 4.2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\subset\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be compact open subsets with
\mathcal{A}
stable under multiplication by
-1
. Let
(\bullet,R)
be as in Notation
2.1.5
, and let
f:\mathcal{B}\to R
be a
\bullet
-function.

Let
\psi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}:\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to R
be the function

\psi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f(y)&\text{ if }g\in\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x&y\\
&1&pz\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }x\in\mathcal{A},\ y\in\mathcal{B},\ z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
0&\text{ otherwise, }\end{array}\right.

and let
\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}:\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to R
be the function

\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f(y)&\text{ if }g\in\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&y&x\\
&1&z\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }x\in\mathcal{A},\ y\in\mathcal{B},\ z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
0&\text{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

Define matrices

(4.2.2)

u_{0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&\\
&&-1\\
&1&\end{smallmatrix}\right),\qquad u=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&1\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)u_{0}.

Lemma 4.2.3
.

In the set-up of Definition
4.2.1
, we have
u_{0}\cdot[\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}]=-[\psi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}]
in
\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R)
.

Proof.

Let
\xi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}:\overline{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\backslash G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to R
be defined by

\xi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f(y)&\text{ if }g\in\overline{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x&y\\
&1&0\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }x\in\mathcal{A},\ y\in\mathcal{B}\\
0&\text{ otherwise. }\end{array}\right.

As this is left-invariant under
\overline{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, its image in
\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R)
is 0. The result thus follows if we show

(4.2.4)

u_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}=\xi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}-\psi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}.

First note that:

Claim 4.2.5
.

We have

\overline{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
&1&0\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})u_{0}^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
&1&\\
&\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\sqcup\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\mathcal{A}&\mathcal{B}\\
&1&p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

Proof of claim:
The Bruhat decomposition tells us
\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\overline{B}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\operatorname{Iw}_{2}^{-}\sqcup\overline{B}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\operatorname{Iw}_{2}^{-}
, where
\operatorname{Iw}_{2}^{-}=\{g\in\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):g\hskip 2.0pt(\mathrm{mod}\hskip 2.0ptp)\in\overline{B}_{2}(\mathbb{F}_{p})\}
. Via Iwahori decomposition (of the form
NT\overline{N}
in the first factor, and
\overline{N}TN
in the second) we see that

\GL_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})=\overline{B}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}&1\\
-1&\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&\\
\mathbb{Z}_{p}&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\sqcup\overline{B}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

The claim follows by considering this computation in the bottom-right
2\times 2
square in
\GL_{3}
.

Now, since
u_{0}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&y&-x\\
&1&-z\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)u_{0}^{-1}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x&y\\
&1&\\
&z&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and
\mathcal{A}
is stable under multiplication by
-1
, we have

\Big(u_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}\Big)(g)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}f(y)&\text{ if }g\in\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})u_{0}^{-1}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&x&y\\
&1&\\
&z&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\text{ with }x\in\mathcal{A},\ y\in\mathcal{B},\ z\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
0&\text{ otherwise, }\end{array}\right.

From the definitions and claim, we see
\xi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}=u_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}+\psi_{\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B},f}
, yielding (
4.2.4
) and the lemma.
∎

Until the end of §
4.2
, we specialise to the setting where
R=\mathcal{O}_{L}
. We introduce the following notation: for any map
f\colon\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
, let
f^{\iota}
denote the function given by
f^{\iota}(x)=f(-x)
for any
x\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
.

Definition 4.2.6
.

If
f\colon(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
is any function, let

\psi_{f}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left[\psi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times},f}\right]\in\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L}).

be as in Definition
4.2.1
, where we lift
f
to a smooth function on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
under the projection
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}\twoheadrightarrow(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
. Then for
\mu\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*})\right)
, let
5
5

5

The involution
(-)^{\iota}
here may seem unnatural, but it is an artifact of our conventions; ultimately, putting it here multiplies the measure
\mathscr{L}(\mu)
in Proposition
4.2.7
by the Dirac measure
[-1]
. This gives cleaner comparison to
[
LW21
]
.

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu,-):\operatorname{Maps}((\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times},\mathcal{O}_{L})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L},

\displaystyle f

\displaystyle\longmapsto\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\mu,-\psi_{f^{\iota}}).

It is standard (see e.g.
[
RJW25
, Prop. 3.15]
) that
\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu)
can be interpreted as an element of
\mathcal{O}_{L}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}]
. We have natural projection maps

\operatorname{Norm}^{n+1}_{n}:\mathcal{O}_{L}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}]\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}].

The following reinterpretation of
[
LW21
]
relates these constructions to
p
-adic measures.

Proposition 4.2.7
.

We have
\operatorname{Norm}^{n+1}_{n}(\mathscr{L}_{n+1}(\mu))=\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu)
. Thus there is an
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-linear map

\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*})\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{L}[\![\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}]\!]\cong\varprojlim_{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}]

\displaystyle\mu

\displaystyle\longmapsto\mathscr{L}(\mu)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big(\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu)\big)_{n\geqslant 1}.

Proof.

Recall the map
\rho_{\mathrm{Iw}}^{0}
and operator
U_{p,1}
from §
3.3
. For ease of notation, let
\phi\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\rho_{\operatorname{Iw}}^{0}(\mu)\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{Ind}^{G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{\operatorname{Iw}}\mathcal{O}_{L}))
. By Lemma
3.3.1
, we know
\phi
is fixed by
U_{p,1}
. This can be considered as a class in
\mathrm{H}^{2}_{\mathrm{c}}(X_{G,K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},\mathcal{O}_{L})
, yielding an input to
[
LW21
, §6.2]
; we will directly compare
\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu)
with the elements
{}_{c}\Xi_{n}^{[0,0]}(\phi,\Phi^{(p)})
constructed there, and use Corollary 6.12
op.cit.
to conclude.

Let
f:(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}\to\mathcal{O}_{L}
be any function. Recall

\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}=\operatorname{ch}\big(\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cdot\operatorname{Iw})=\varphi_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbbm{1}},

the latter in the notation of Definition
4.2.1
. We first claim that as elements of
\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
, we have

(4.2.8)

-\psi_{f^{\iota}}=\sum_{b\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}f(-b)\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot[\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}],

where
\delta_{b}=\operatorname{diag}(-b,1,1)\in H(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. Indeed,

t^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}=t^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbbm{1}}=\varphi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbbm{1}},

and then Lemma
4.2.3
implies

u_{0}t^{n}\cdot[\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}]=-[\psi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbbm{1}}]=-\left[\operatorname{ch}\left(\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]\in\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L}).

One then checks that

(4.2.9)

f(-b)\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot[\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}]=-f(-b)\left[\operatorname{ch}\left(\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}&b+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&p\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]=-[\psi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},b+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},f^{\iota}}]

as elements of
\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
. Equation (
4.2.8
) follows after taking the sum.

Recall
U(p^{n})^{\circ}
from §
3.4
, and
\phi=\rho^{0}_{\operatorname{Iw}}(\mu)
; and consider the class

\iota^{*}_{n}(\phi)\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\Big(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}\mathcal{O}_{L})\Big)

defined by

(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})\mapsto\mu(\iota(d),[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}ut^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}),

where as usual we view
H\subset G
block diagonally. In classical language,
\iota^{*}_{n}(\phi)
is the Iwahori level class for
G
, twisted by the action of
ut^{n}
and pulled back to
H
. Finally, let

\nu_{1}:H\to\GL_{1},\qquad(h_{1},h_{2})\mapsto\tfrac{\det h_{1}}{h_{2}}

be the map from
[
LW21
, Def. 4.2]
, and let
[f]\in\operatorname{H}^{0}(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}\mathcal{O}_{L}))
be the class given by

([h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})\mapsto f(\nu_{1}(h_{p})|\!|\nu_{1}(h^{p}h_{p})|\!|)

where
|\!|-|\!|
denotes the finite part of the adelic norm.

An explicit computation (using the claim above) shows that

(4.2.10)

\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}(\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f])=\Psi_{n}(\mu,-\psi_{f^{\iota}})

since
\{\delta_{b}\}
are a set of representatives of
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}/U(p^{n})^{\circ}
. Indeed, for
x\in I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
,
b\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
,
d\in D_{H}
, and
h_{\infty}\in H(\mathbb{R})
, we have
\nu_{1}(\delta_{b})|\!|\nu_{1}(x\delta_{b})|\!|=-b
, and hence

\displaystyle\left(\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f]\right)(d,[h_{\infty}],x,\delta_{b})

\displaystyle=f(-b)\mu(\iota(d),[h_{\infty}],x,\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})

\displaystyle=f(-b)\mu(\iota(d),h\cdot[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],hx,h\cdot\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})

\displaystyle=\mu(h^{-1}\cdot\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,f(-b)\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})

\displaystyle=\Psi_{n}(\mu,-[\psi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},b+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},f^{\iota}}])(d,[h_{\infty}],x,1).

Here
h\in H(\mathbb{Q})
is any element such that
h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}]=[h_{\infty}]
and
x^{-1}hx\in K^{p}_{H}U(p^{n})^{\circ}
. The second equality follows the fact that
\mu
is invariant under right-translation by
K^{p}_{H}
and that
U(p^{n})^{\circ}
fixes
\delta_{b}ut^{n}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}
, and the third equality follows from the
G(\mathbb{Q})
-equivariance of
\mu
. The final equality follows from (
4.2.9
). We see that

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}(\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f])(d,[h_{\infty}],x,1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}(\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f])(d,[h_{\infty}],x,\delta_{b})

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}}\Psi_{n}(\mu,-[\psi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},b+p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},f^{\iota}}]))(d,[h_{\infty}],x,1)

\displaystyle=\Psi_{n}(\mu,-\psi_{f^{\iota}})(d,[h_{\infty}],x,1)

where the last equality follows from the bilinearity of
\Psi_{n}
. The equality (
4.2.10
) now follows because both sides are determined by their values on
(d,[h_{\infty}],x,1)
(using Notation
4.1.1
(iii)).

We now see that

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{n}(\mu,f)

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{n}(\mu,-\psi_{f^{\iota}}),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\langle\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}(\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f]),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\langle\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi)\cup[f],\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\circ}}\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}

(4.2.11)

\displaystyle=\langle\iota_{n}^{*}(\phi),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\circ}}\cup[f]\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\circ}}.

This now bears direct comparison to analogous objects constructed in
[
LW21
, §6.2]
;
in the notation
op. cit
., this is precisely the element
{}_{c}\Xi^{[0,0]}_{n}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}[(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}]
evaluated at
f
. Precisely, the pairing defining
{}_{c}\Xi_{n}^{[0,0]}
there is a pushforward under
\iota_{n}
; using adjointness of pushforward and pullback, we see that pairing equals (
4.2.11
).

Now, the result follows from
[
LW21
, Corollary 6.12]
, which shows the
{{}_{c}\Xi^{[0,0]}_{n}}
fit into a
p
-adic measure as
n
varies. Here we use crucially that
U_{p,1}
fixes
\phi
.
∎

4.3.
p
-adic periods attached to
p
-arithmetic cohomology classes

We will also consider other values of this evaluation map. We begin with the following analogue of
\Psi_{n}
, where we replace
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
with
U_{1}(p^{n})
and specialise the Steinberg variable.

Definition 4.3.1
.

Let
\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}\in\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
denote the Iwahori-invariant vector represented by the characteristic function of
\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\operatorname{Iw}
. For any integer
m\geqslant 1
, we let

\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},m}(\mu)\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}_{U_{1}(p^{m})}\mathcal{O}_{L})\right)

denote the element satisfying

\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},m}(\mu)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})=\mu(\iota(d),[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}u_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}})

for
d\in D_{H}
,
[h_{\infty}]\in\pi_{0}(H(\mathbb{R}))
,
h^{p}\in H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
h_{p}\in H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. This is well-defined, since
u_{0}^{-1}U_{1}(p^{m})u_{0}\subset\operatorname{Iw}
for all
m
. Note also that
\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},m}(\mu)
is nothing but
\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu)
considered at level
U_{1}(p^{m})
, so this class has no significant dependence on
m
. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},m}(\mu),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{m},U_{1}(p^{m})}\right\rangle_{U_{1}(p^{m})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{res}(\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu)),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{m},U_{1}(p^{m})}\right\rangle_{U_{1}(p^{m})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu),\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{m},U_{1}(p^{m})}\right)\right\rangle_{U_{1}(p)}=\left\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U_{1}(p)}\right\rangle_{U_{1}(p)}.

In particular, the quantity

(4.3.2)

\mathscr{P}(\mu)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},m}(\mu),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{m},U_{1}(p^{m})}\right\rangle_{U_{1}(p^{m})}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}

is independent of
m
.

We have the following relation. Let
\xi_{n}=\operatorname{ch}\left(\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&p\mathbb{Z}_{p}&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)=\varphi_{p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p},p\mathbb{Z}_{p},\mathbbm{1}}\in\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
.

Lemma 4.3.3
.

We have

\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{n})=\mathscr{P}(\mu)

for any
n\geqslant 1
.

Proof.

Let
\{z\}
be a set of representatives for
p\mathbb{Z}_{p}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Then, we calculate that

(4.3.4)

\sum_{z}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&z&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot u_{0}\cdot\xi_{n}=u_{0}t\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}=tu_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}=u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1}.

We consider two Hecke operators:

V_{p}=[U(p)^{\lozenge}\cdot t\cdot U_{1}(p)],\quad\quad V_{p}^{t}=[U_{1}(p)\cdot t^{-1}\cdot U(p)^{\lozenge}].

Note that
V_{p}^{t}\cdot\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{1}
.

We first calculate that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{1}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1})

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{1}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1}),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U(p)^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p)^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{res}_{U(p)^{\lozenge}}^{U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}\Psi_{1}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1}),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}\right\rangle_{U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}\Psi_{n}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{n}),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}\right\rangle_{U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Psi_{n}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{n},),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{n}).

For the second equality we have used that
\operatorname{tr}_{U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}^{U(p)^{\lozenge}}\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p)^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n})}=\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U(p)^{\lozenge}}
, and the third uses (
4.3.4
). This shows independence of
n
and allows us to assume
n=1
.

Another calculation shows:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{1}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1})

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{1}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1}),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U(p)^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p)^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\langle V_{p}\cdot\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U(p)^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p)^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu),V_{p}^{t}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U(p)^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U_{1}(p)}

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi_{\operatorname{Iw},1}(\mu),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{1},U_{1}(p)}\rangle_{U_{1}(p)}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{P}(\mu)

where for the second equality we have used
tu_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\operatorname{Iw}}=u_{0}\cdot\xi_{1}
, and the penultimate equality uses
V_{p}^{t}\cdot\Phi_{1}=\Phi_{1}
and equivariance of the Eisenstein classes.
∎

4.4.
p
-smooth representations

The rest of this section is concerned with an abstract exceptional zero formula. One of the key ingredients will be an analogue of the above evaluation maps on
\mathrm{H}^{1}
rather than
\mathrm{H}^{0}
. To define this, we need some abstract notions. We return to the case of allowing
R
to be any
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra. For any integer
n\geqslant 1
, let

\widehat{G}_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{ker}(G(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})\to G(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z}))\subset G(\mathbb{Q}).

Definition 4.4.1
.

Let
M
be a left
R[G(\mathbb{Q})]
-module. We define the “
p
-smooth part” to be

M^{p\operatorname{-sm}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigcup_{n\geqslant 1}M^{\widehat{G}_{n}}\subset M.

Lemma 4.4.2
.

The
G(\mathbb{Q})
-action on
M
preserves
M^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
(which is thus a
G(\mathbb{Q})
-representation).

Proof.

If
m\in M^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
, then
m
is fixed by some
\widehat{G}_{N_{m}}
, hence by
\widehat{G}_{N}
for all
N\geqslant N_{m}
.

Let
g\in G(\mathbb{Q})
. We have an analogue of the Cartan decomposition

G(\mathbb{Q})=\bigsqcup_{a\geqslant b\geqslant c}G(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})t_{abc}G(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}),\qquad t_{abc}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{a}&&\\
&p^{b}&\\
&&p^{c}\end{smallmatrix}\right);

write
g=g_{1}t_{abc}g_{2}
in this decomposition. For
n>a
, we have
t_{abc}^{-1}\widehat{G}_{n}t_{abc}\subset\widehat{G}_{n-a}
.

Let
N>N_{m}+a
, and
h\in\widehat{G}_{N}
. As each
\widehat{G}_{n}\triangleleft G(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)})
is normal, and by the above, there exists
h^{\prime}\in\widehat{G}_{N-a}\subset\widehat{G}_{N_{m}}
such that
hg=gh^{\prime}
. Then
h\cdot(g\cdot m)=gh^{\prime}\cdot m=g\cdot m
, since
\widehat{G}_{N_{m}}
fixes
m
. In particular

g\cdot m\in M^{\widehat{G}_{N}}\subset M^{p\operatorname{-sm}}.\qed

4.5.
A preliminary map

Recall
\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)=\operatorname{St}_{2}^{\operatorname{sm}}(R)
from Definition
2.1.3
. The goal of this section is to construct maps

\Xi_{n}\colon\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}\to\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)

satisfying Proposition
4.5.4
.

4.5.1.
Definition of the maps

We first define the maps and show they are well-defined. Let

\phi_{n}=\left[\operatorname{ch}\left(\overline{P}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\right)\right]\in\operatorname{St}_{2}(R).

Definition 4.5.1
.

Define

\Xi_{n}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=F(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}).

Here we use the same conventions as when defining
\Psi_{n}
in Definition
4.1.2
; in particular we write
[h_{\infty}]=h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}]
,
h^{p}=hxk^{p}
and
h_{p}=hk_{p}
. The map bears strong comparison to
\Psi_{n}
, but we now impose the weaker assumption of
\widehat{G}_{n}
-invariance, are working with
\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*}
rather than
\operatorname{St}(R)^{*}
, and fix the Steinberg element to be
u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}
. As with
\Psi_{n}
, we must show this is well-defined, as
h
is only well-defined up to
\Gamma(p^{n})^{\lozenge}_{x}
. First we note:

Lemma 4.5.2
.

If
k_{p}\in U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
, then
k_{p}u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}=u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
\overline{P}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)=\overline{P}_{2}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)u_{0}^{-1}k_{p}u_{0}
. We write
k_{p}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a&b&0\\
c&d&0\\
0&0&e\end{smallmatrix}\right)
with
a,e\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
,
d\equiv 1\pmod{p^{n}}
and
b,c\equiv 0\pmod{p^{n}}
. Let
\delta=ad-bc\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
. For the inclusion
\supset
, note that for each
x\in p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
we have

\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&x\\
&1&y\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)u_{0}^{-1}k_{p}u_{0}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}a-cx&0&0\\
-cy&e&0\\
-c&0&\tfrac{\delta}{a-cx}\end{smallmatrix}\right)\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&0&\tfrac{-b+dx}{a-cx}\\
0&1&\tfrac{\delta y}{e(a-cx)}\\
0&0&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\in\overline{P}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&p^{n}\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&1&\mathbb{Z}_{p}\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right).

For the other inclusion, it is enough to consider the above with
k_{p}^{-1}
instead of
k_{p}
.
∎

Lemma 4.5.3
.

The function
\Xi_{n}(F)\in\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)
is well-defined, independent of the choice of representatives
I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
, and
H(\mathbb{Q})
-invariant.

Proof.

This is directly analogous to Lemmas
4.1.3
and
4.1.5
, replacing the
U(p^{n})
-invariance of
\varphi
with Lemma
4.5.2
. We note that by Lemma
3.4.1
the elements
\gamma
and
h_{i}
considered in Lemma
4.1.3
actually lie in
\widehat{G}_{n}
, so the arguments with
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariance in Lemma
4.1.3
transfer
mutatis mutandis
to the present setting.
∎

4.5.2.
Compatibility in
n
: statement

Now we consider variation in
n
. The set of representatives
I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
can be chosen independently of
n
, so we may assume
I(p^{n})^{\lozenge}=I(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}=:I
.

Proposition 4.5.4
.

For
n\geqslant 1
, the maps
\Xi_{n}
satisfy the following two properties:

(a)

One has a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n+1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}}

where
\operatorname{res}
and
\operatorname{tr}
denote the natural restriction and trace maps.

(b)

Let
t=\operatorname{diag}(p,1,1)
. One has a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n+1}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(H(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{H,c}(\operatorname{Ind}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})}R)\right)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{n+1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t\cdot}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Xi_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{res}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}}

4.5.3.
Compatibility in
n
: proofs

We will prove each part of Proposition
4.5.4
separately.

Lemma 4.5.5
.

The maps
\Xi_{n}
satisfy property (a) of Proposition
4.5.4
.

Proof.

Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}
. Let
d\in D_{H}
,
h_{\infty}\in H(\mathbb{R})
,
h^{p}\in H(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
and
h_{p}\in H(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and let
h\in H(\mathbb{Q})
,
x\in I
,
k^{p}\in K^{p}_{H}
and
k_{p}\in U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}\subset U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
be such that:

[h_{\infty}]=h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],\;h^{p}=hxk^{p},\;h_{p}=hk_{p}.

Then we have:

\left(\operatorname{res}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\circ\ \Xi_{n}\right)(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})=F(h^{-1}\cdot\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}).

Let

J_{n}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&-p^{n}b&\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right):b\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\right\}\subset H(\mathbb{Q})_{+}\cap\widehat{G}_{n}\subset H(\mathbb{A}).

Considering
J_{n}\subset U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
gives a set of representatives for
[U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})]/U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}
. In particular,

\left(\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\circ\ \Xi_{n+1}\right)(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})=\sum_{v\in J_{n}}\Xi_{n+1}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}v).

We compute

\displaystyle\Xi_{n+1}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}v)

\displaystyle=\Xi_{n+1}(F)(d,h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],hxk^{p},hk_{p}v)

\displaystyle=\Xi_{n+1}(F)(d,hv[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],hv\cdot(v^{p})^{-1}xk^{p},hvk_{p}^{\prime}),

where
k_{p}^{\prime}=v^{-1}k_{p}v\in v^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}v=U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}
. Note that
(v^{p})^{-1}x=v^{-1}xv_{p}
represents the same double coset as
x
. Via Lemma
4.5.3
, we may compute
\Xi_{n+1}(F)
with respect to the set of representatives
I^{\prime}=\{(v^{p})^{-1}x:x\in I\}
, giving

\displaystyle\Xi_{n+1}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}v)

\displaystyle=F(v^{-1}h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],(v^{p})^{-1}x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1})

\displaystyle=F(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,vu_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1}),

where in the second equality we use
\widehat{G}_{n}
-invariance of
F
.

A direct computation shows

\sum_{v}(u_{0}^{-1}vu_{0})\cdot\phi_{n+1}=\sum_{b}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&&p^{n}b\\
&1&\\
&&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot\phi_{n+1}=\phi_{n}.

In particular

\sum_{v}vu_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1}=u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n}.

Combining everything, we find

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{tr}_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\circ\Xi_{n+1}\right)(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{v}F(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,vu_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1})

\displaystyle=F(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=\left(\operatorname{res}_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}^{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\circ\Xi_{n}\right)(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}),

as required.
∎

Lemma 4.5.6
.

The maps
\Xi_{n}
satisfy property (b) of Proposition
4.5.4
.

Proof.

Let
I=I(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}=\{x_{i}\}
be a set of representatives. Then
I^{\prime}=\{x_{i}^{\prime}=t^{-1}x_{i}\}
is a set of representatives (in
\prod_{\ell\neq p}H(\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
) for the level
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
. Let
F\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}
. Then, with the same notation at the start of the proof of Lemma
4.5.5
,

\displaystyle\Xi_{n+1}(t\cdot F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p})

\displaystyle=\Xi_{n+1}(t\cdot F)(d,h[H(\mathbb{R})_{+}],hxk^{p},hk_{p})

\displaystyle=(t\cdot F)(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1})

\displaystyle=F((ht)^{-1}d,t^{-1}[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],t^{-1}x,t^{-1}u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n+1}).

Note
t
commutes with
u_{0}
and
t^{-1}\cdot\phi_{n+1}=\phi_{n}
, whilst
t^{-1}
fixes
[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}]
. Also,
t^{-1}k_{p}t\in U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
. This thus equals

(4.5.7)

\displaystyle F((ht)^{-1}d,[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],t^{-1}x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=\Xi_{n}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],ht(t^{-1}x)k^{p},ht\cdot t^{-1}k_{p}t)

\displaystyle=\Xi_{n}(F)(d,[h_{\infty}],h^{p},h_{p}t)

as required. Note that in (
4.5.7
) we compute
\Xi_{n}
with respect to the representatives
I^{\prime}=\{t^{-1}x:x\in I\}
, which is valid by Lemma
4.5.3
.
∎

4.6.
Big evaluation maps

We now define the big evaluation maps. Firstly, let

\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}\colon\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}\to R

denote the
R
-linear map given by

\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}(F)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

noting
u_{0}
normalises
\widehat{G}_{n}
and recalling
\Phi_{n}
from Notation
3.5.3
. We emphasise here that
u_{0}
is acting on
F
as an element of
G(\mathbb{Q})
, so that the following lemma holds. Recall
\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}
from Definition
4.1.7
, and the
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-equivariant morphism
\operatorname{pr}\colon\operatorname{St}_{1}(R)\otimes\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)\to\operatorname{St}(R)
.

Lemma 4.6.1
.

For any
\mu\in\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}(R)^{*}))
and
\xi\in\operatorname{St}_{1}(R)^{U(p^{n})}
, we have

\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}\big(\mu,\ u_{0}\cdot\operatorname{pr}(\xi\otimes\phi_{n})\big)=\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}(\mu_{\xi}),

where
\mu_{\xi}\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{\widehat{G}_{n}}
is the element

\mu_{\xi}(d,[g_{\infty}],g^{p},\phi)=\mu(d,[g_{\infty}],g^{p},\operatorname{pr}(\xi\otimes\phi))

for
d\in D_{G}
,
[g_{\infty}]\in\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R}))
,
g^{p}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
, and
\phi\in\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)
.

Proof.

With notation from Definition
4.5.1
with
F=u_{0}\cdot\mu_{\xi}
, we compute that

\displaystyle(u_{0}\cdot\mu_{\xi})(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=\mu_{\xi}(u_{0}^{-1}h^{-1}\iota(d),u_{0}^{-1}[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],u_{0}^{-1}x,\phi_{n})

\displaystyle=\mu(u_{0}^{-1}h^{-1}\iota(d),u_{0}^{-1}[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],u_{0}^{-1}x,\operatorname{pr}(\xi\otimes\phi_{n}))

\displaystyle=\mu(h^{-1}\iota(d),[G(\mathbb{R})_{+}],x,u_{0}\cdot\operatorname{pr}(\xi\otimes\phi_{n}))

where the last equality uses the
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariance of
\mu
. From the definition of
\Psi_{n}
(Definition
4.1.2
), we therefore see that
\Psi_{n}(\mu,u_{0}\cdot\operatorname{pr}(\xi\otimes\phi_{n}))=\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot\mu_{\xi})
, which implies the claim.
∎

Lemma 4.6.2
.

We have
\widetilde{\Xi}_{n+1}(F)=\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}(F)
. In particular, there is a map

\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}\colon\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}}\to R

defined by
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varinjlim_{n}\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}
.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{n+1}(F)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n+1}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{tr}\big(\Xi_{n+1}(u_{0}\cdot F)\big),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{res}\big(\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F)\big),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})}\right)\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{tr}(\Phi_{n+1}),U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}})\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}.

Here the second and fourth equalities use that restriction and trace are adjoint under the Poincaré pairing, the third equality is by Proposition
4.5.4
(a), and the last equality is trace compatibility (
3.5.2
) of Eisenstein classes. Now, for
b,c\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
let

(4.6.3)

v_{b,c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&0\\
p^{n}b&1+p^{n}c\end{matrix}\right).

Then
\{v_{b,c}\times 1:b,c\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\}\subset U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}
is a set of representatives for
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}/U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}\cap U_{1}(p^{n+1})
. We compute

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}(\Phi_{p,n+1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}v_{-b,-c}\cdot\operatorname{ch}(p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p},1+p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\operatorname{ch}(p^{n}(b+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}),1+p^{n}(c+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))=\Phi_{p,n}.

We conclude that

\widetilde{\Xi}_{n+1}(F)=\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}=\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}(F),

as required. The second statement is immediate.
∎

Lemma 4.6.4
.

The map
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}
is
t^{\mathbb{Z}}
-equivariant, where the target has the trivial action.

Proof.

It suffices to prove
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(t\cdot F)=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(F)
. Recall that
t
commutes with
u_{0}
. Let
F
be fixed by
\widehat{G}_{n}
. Then

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(t\cdot F)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\Xi}_{n+1}(t\cdot F)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n+1}(t\cdot u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle t\cdot\operatorname{res}\big(\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F)\big),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{res}\big(\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F)\big),t^{-1}\cdot\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n+1},U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\operatorname{res}\big(\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F)),\mathcal{E}_{t^{-1}\cdot\Phi_{n+1},t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}\right\rangle_{t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathcal{E}_{t^{-1}\cdot\Phi_{n+1},t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t}\right)\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\operatorname{tr}(t^{-1}\cdot\Phi_{n+1}),U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}},

where the third equality follows from Proposition
4.5.4
(b), the fifth equality being (
3.5.1
) and the last again being (
3.5.2
). Now, a set of representatives of
U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}/t^{-1}U(p^{n+1})^{\lozenge}t
is
\{v_{b,c}\times 1:b\in\mathbb{Z}/p^{2}\mathbb{Z},c\in\mathbb{Z}/p\mathbb{Z}\}
, for
v_{b,c}
as in (
4.6.3
). We calculate that

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\left(t^{-1}\cdot\Phi_{p,n+1}\right)=\sum_{b,c}v_{-b,-c}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p^{-1}&\\
&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\cdot\Phi_{p,n+1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}v_{-b,-c}\operatorname{ch}(p^{n+2}\mathbb{Z}_{p},1+p^{n+1}\mathbb{Z}_{p})

\displaystyle=\sum_{b,c}\operatorname{ch}(p^{n}(b+p^{2}\mathbb{Z}_{p}),1+p^{n}(c+p\mathbb{Z}_{p}))=\Phi_{p,n}.

Plugging this in, we find

\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(t\cdot F)=\left\langle\Xi_{n}(u_{0}\cdot F),\mathcal{E}_{\Phi_{n},U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}\right\rangle_{U(p^{n})^{\lozenge}}=\widetilde{\Xi}_{n}(F)=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(F),

as required.
∎

The following is the main definition of this subsection.

Definition 4.6.5
.

Define the
big evaluation map

\operatorname{Ev}^{\operatorname{big}}_{R}\colon\operatorname{H}^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}})\to R

as
\operatorname{Ev}^{\operatorname{big}}_{R}([c])=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(c[t])
, where
c
is any choice of cocycle representing
[c]
.

Lemma 4.6.6
.

The map
\operatorname{Ev}^{\operatorname{big}}_{R}([c])
is well-defined (i.e., independent of the choice of cocycle
c
).

Proof.

Any other choice of cocycle
c^{\prime}
differs by a coboundary
b[g]=g\cdot F-F
for some
F\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
. As
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(b[t])=0
by Lemma
4.6.4
, we see
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(c[t])=\widetilde{\Xi}_{\infty}(c^{\prime}[t])
, as required.
∎

4.7.
Abstract
\mathcal{L}
-invariants and the exceptional zero formula

We now place ourselves in one of the situations described in Notation
2.1.5
. We will often drop “
\operatorname{sm}
” from the notation. Recall the cocycle
c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}\in Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\operatorname{St}_{1}^{\bullet}(R))
from (
2.2.4
), where
\lambda\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\bullet}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times},R)
. Define a map

\Upsilon^{\bullet}_{\lambda}\colon\operatorname{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R)^{*}))\longrightarrow Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*}))

by

\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\bullet}(\mu)[z]\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left((d,g_{\infty},g^{p},\phi)\mapsto\mu\left(d,g_{\infty},g^{p},\operatorname{pr}\Big(c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}[z]\otimes\phi\Big)\right)\right)

where
z\in G(\mathbb{Q})
,
d\in D_{G}
,
g_{\infty}\in\pi_{0}(G(\mathbb{R}))
,
g^{p}\in G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
,
\phi\in\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)
.

Remark 4.7.1
.

We can alternatively describe
\Upsilon^{\bullet}_{\lambda}
as follows. Recall the cocycle

\Delta^{\bullet}_{1,\lambda}\in Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R),\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R)))

from Remark
2.4.2
. From the definitions, we see
\Upsilon^{\bullet}_{\lambda}(\mu)
is the cup product
\mu\cup\Delta^{\bullet}_{1,\lambda}|_{G(\mathbb{Q})}
composed with the natural map
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R)^{*})\otimes\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R),\operatorname{St}^{\bullet}(R))\to\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})
.

Later, in §
5.3
, because of this description and in line with our wider notation, we will denote this map
\Upsilon_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}
. To ease notation, in this section we will continue to omit the subscript 1.

Lemma 4.7.2
.

For
\bullet=\operatorname{sm}
, the map
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{\lambda}
lands in
Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}})
.

Proof.

As
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}_{1}(R)
is a smooth
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
-module,
c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}[z]
is fixed by
\widehat{G}_{n(z)}
for some
n(z)\gg 0
(depending on
z
). For
g\in\widehat{G}_{n(z)}
we thus have

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mu)[z](gd,gg_{\infty},gg^{p},g\cdot\phi)

\displaystyle=\mu\left(gd,gg_{\infty},gg^{p},\operatorname{pr}\left(c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}[z]\otimes g\cdot\phi\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mu\left(gd,gg_{\infty},gg^{p},\operatorname{pr}\left(g\cdot c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}[z]\otimes g\cdot\phi\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mu\left(d,g_{\infty},g^{p},\operatorname{pr}\left(c_{1,\lambda}^{\bullet}[z]\otimes\phi\right)\right)=\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mu)[z](d,g_{\infty},g^{p},\phi),

where the penultimate equality is
G(\mathbb{Q})
-invariance of
\mu
. In particular for every
\mu
and
z
,
\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mu)[z]
is fixed by
\widehat{G}_{n(z)}
for some
n(z)\gg 0
; so
\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mu)[z]\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(R)^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
.
∎

Remark 4.7.3
.

In particular, we may apply
\operatorname{Ev}^{\operatorname{big}}_{R}
to the image of
\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}
. This lemma captures the fundamental necessity of developing the theory of
p
-smooth coefficients: the space
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(-)^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
is an intermediary between
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(-)
and
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(-)^{G(\mathbb{Q})}
, that is large enough to capture the image of
\Upsilon_{\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}
, but small enough that we can construct evaluation maps on
\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(-)^{p\operatorname{-sm}}
.

We consider the following situation. Let
\mu^{\operatorname{cts}}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{cts}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*})\right)
, and let

\mu_{s}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*})\right),\quad\quad\mu^{\operatorname{la}}\in\operatorname{H}^{0}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{la}}(L)^{*})\right),

denote the images of
\mu^{\operatorname{cts}}
under the natural maps to these cohomology groups (here
s\geqslant 1
is any integer). Suppose there exists an element
\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\in L
such that the image
[\Upsilon^{\operatorname{la}}_{\lambda}(\mu^{\operatorname{la}})]
of
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{la}}_{\lambda}(\mu^{\operatorname{la}})
in
\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(L)^{*})\right)
is zero, where
\lambda=\operatorname{log}_{p}-\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\operatorname{ord}_{p}
.

Lemma 4.7.4
.

There exists an integer
k\geqslant 1
such that:
p^{k}\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
, and for all
s\geqslant 1
the image of
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{p^{k}\lambda}(\mu_{s})
in
\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}}\right)
is zero.

Proof.

Let
k_{0}\geqslant 1
be such that
p^{k_{0}}\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\in\mathcal{O}_{L}
, so that
p^{k_{0}}\lambda\in\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{cts}}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}^{\times},\mathcal{O}_{L})
. By the commutative diagram (
2.4.1
), combined with Remarks
2.4.2
and
4.7.1
, for any
r\geqslant k_{0}
the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{s}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\operatorname{cts}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{cts}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{cts}}(L)^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(L)^{*}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu^{\operatorname{la}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{la}}(L)^{*}))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{p^{r}\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{p^{r}\lambda}^{\operatorname{cts}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces-\otimes\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{p^{r}\lambda}^{\operatorname{cts}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Upsilon_{p^{r}\lambda}^{\operatorname{la}}}

commutes. (Here we compose
\Upsilon_{p^{r}\lambda}^{\bullet}
with the quotient map
[-]:Z^{1}\to\mathrm{H}^{1}
).

First take
r=k_{0}
. By assumption, the image of
\mu^{\operatorname{la}}
along the bottom diagonal arrow is zero, and it follows that
[\Upsilon^{\operatorname{cts}}_{p^{k_{0}}\lambda}(\mu^{\operatorname{cts}})]
is zero after tensoring with
L
; in particular, it is torsion in the cohomology group
\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*})\right)
. Since the module
\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L})
is flawless (see
[
Geh21
, Definition 2.1, Theorem 2.6]
), this cohomology group is a finitely-generated
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-module (
[
Geh21
, Proposition 3.2(a)]
). This means that there exists an integer
k^{\prime}\geqslant 1
such that
p^{k^{\prime}}
kills
[\Upsilon^{\operatorname{cts}}_{p^{k_{0}}\lambda}(\mu^{\operatorname{cts}})]
.

Now set
k=k_{0}+k^{\prime}
. By the above
[\Upsilon_{p^{k}\lambda}^{\operatorname{cts}}(\mu^{\operatorname{cts}})]=0
. Using the diagram above with
r=k
, we see that for any
s\geqslant 1
, we have

(4.7.5)

0=[\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{p^{k}\lambda}(\mu_{s})]\in\operatorname{H}^{1}\left(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*})\right).

It remains to show that it is zero in the cohomology with
p
-smooth coefficients.

By (
4.7.5
) there exists
m\in\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*}))
such that

\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{p^{k}\lambda}(\mu_{s})[z]=z\cdot m-m.

We must show
m
is
p
-smooth, i.e., there exists
n\gg 0
such that
\widehat{G}_{n}
fixes
m
, as then
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{p^{k}\lambda}(\mu_{s})
is a coboundary in
Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})^{*})^{p\operatorname{-sm}})
. For this, note that
\mu_{s}
is fixed by all of
G(\mathbb{Q})
by definition. To define
\Upsilon^{\operatorname{sm}}_{p^{k}\lambda}(\mu_{s})
, we cup with
\Delta_{1,p^{k}\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}\in Z^{1}(G(\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\operatorname{Hom}_{R}(\operatorname{St}_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s}),\operatorname{St}(\mathcal{O}_{L}/\varpi^{s})))
(after restriction to
G(\mathbb{Q})
). As
\Delta_{1,p^{k}\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}
is defined on
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and we are working with coefficients in finite characteristic, by
[
Hey21
, §3]
it is continuous. In particular it vanishes on some open neighbourhood of the identity; so its restriction to
G(\mathbb{Q})
must vanish on
\widehat{G}_{n}
for
n\gg 0
. It follows that
\mu_{s}\cup\Delta_{1,p^{k}\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}|_{G(\mathbb{Q})}
vanishes on
\widehat{G}_{n}
, so
\Upsilon_{p^{k}\lambda}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mu_{s})
does too. Thus
m
is
p
-smooth and we are done.
∎

The main result of this section is the following:

Theorem 4.7.6
.

Let
\mu^{\operatorname{cts}}
be any element of
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{cts}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))
, and let
\mu
(resp.
\mu^{\operatorname{la}}
) be its image in
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{sm}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))
(resp.
\mathrm{H}^{0}(G(\mathbb{Q}),\mathcal{A}_{G,c}(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{la}}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{*}))
. Let
\mathscr{L}(\mu)
(resp.
\mathscr{P}(\mu)
) denote the measure (resp. period) associated with
\mu
as in Proposition
4.2.7
(resp. Definition
4.3.1
).

Let
\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\in L
be any element such that
[\Upsilon_{\log_{p}-\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\operatorname{ord}_{p}}^{\operatorname{la}}(\mu^{\operatorname{la}})]=0
. Then in
L
, we have an equality

\mathscr{L}(\mu,\operatorname{log}_{p})=\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\cdot\mathscr{P}(\mu).

Proof.

Note
\log_{p}
is not a smooth function on
\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
; however, its reduction modulo
\varpi^{s}
is smooth for all
s\geqslant 1
. In particular for fixed
s
,
\log_{p}\hskip 2.0pt(\mathrm{mod}\hskip 2.0pt\varpi^{s})
factors through
(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}\mathbb{Z})^{\times}
for sufficiently large
n
, and then from the definitions

(4.7.7)

\mathscr{L}(\mu,\log_{p})\hskip 2.0pt(\mathrm{mod}\hskip 2.0pt\varpi^{s})=\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}\left(\mu_{s},-\psi_{\log_{p}^{\iota}}\right)=\widetilde{\Psi}_{n}\left(\mu_{s},-\psi_{\log_{p}}\right).

where we have used the fact that
\operatorname{log}_{p}^{\iota}=\operatorname{log}_{p}
. As such, we will first prove the formula modulo
\varpi^{s}
, using the machinery developed above.

Let
k\geqslant 1
be as in Lemma
4.7.4
, and set
\lambda=\operatorname{log}_{p}-\mathcal{L}_{\mu}\operatorname{ord}_{p}
. Fix
s\geqslant 1
and take
