\displaystyle\operatorname{H}^{d}\left(R\Gamma(T(b),\mathcal{O}(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda})\otimes I_{w}(\mathcal{C})^{\operatorname{ord}})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}\left(R\Gamma(T(b),\mathcal{O}(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}+\chi_{w})\otimes\tau_{w}^{-1}\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{ord}})\right)

\displaystyle\cong\tau_{w}^{-1}\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}\left(R\Gamma(T(b),\mathcal{O}(w(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}+\chi_{w}))\otimes\mathcal{C}^{\operatorname{ord}})\right).

Note
\chi_{w}
factors through the natural map
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and coincides with
(w^{-1}w_{0}^{M}w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}})\cdot\rho-\rho
when restricted to
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We claim that
\lambda_{x}|_{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}=w_{0}^{M}w(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}+\chi_{w})|_{T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})}
; then the result follows from Lemma
9.5.2
(as
w_{0}^{M}=(w_{0}^{M})^{-1}
). But, after restricting to
T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we have

w_{0}^{M}w(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}+\chi_{w})=w_{0}^{M}ww_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}+w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho-w_{0}^{M}w\rho=x\widetilde{\lambda}-\rho+x\rho=\lambda_{x},

as required. In the last step we have used the property
w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\rho=-\rho
.
∎

Combining (
9.5.1
), (
9.5.5
) and Lemma
9.5.6
, we obtain a subquotient

(9.5.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{H}^{d-l(x)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x}}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}}.}}

This is
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
-equivariant, where the action on the target is via
\mathcal{S}_{w}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
; recall
\mathcal{S}_{w}
is the ring homomorphism coinciding with
\mathcal{S}
away from
p
, and which at
p
is induced from the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}[T^{+}]

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{O}[T_{M}^{+}]

\displaystyle[t]

\displaystyle\mapsto[wtw^{-1}].

The map on Hecke algebras induced from (
9.5.7
) is the desired transfer map modulo
\varpi^{m}
. To obtain the map in characteristic zero, we pass to the inverse limit over
m
. Let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda_{x})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\operatorname{H}^{d-l(x)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)=\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\varprojlim_{m}\operatorname{H}^{d-l(x)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x}}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}}\right).

Combining everything in this section, we obtain the following:

Proposition 9.5.8
.

Let
p>2n
. One has an
\mathcal{O}
-algebra homomorphism

\mathcal{S}_{w}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda})\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda_{x})

induced by
\mathcal{S}_{w}
.

Proof.

Note that the morphisms in (
9.5.1
) and Lemma
9.5.3
remain surjective after passing to the limit over
m\geqslant 1
, because the modules are finite. Similarly, the sequence in (
9.5.4
) remains exact after passing to the limit over
m\geqslant 1
, for the same reason. Thus (
9.5.7
) remains a subquotient in the limit, implying the existence of the desired morphism of Hecke algebras.
∎

We also note the following:

Proposition 9.5.9
.

With notation as above, the ordinary part
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{K}}^{P},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}
carries a Hecke-equivariant descending filtration

\operatorname{Fil}^{i}\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{K}}^{P},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}

with the properties that:

•

\operatorname{Fil}^{0}\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{K}}^{P},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}=\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{K}}^{P},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}
and
\operatorname{Fil}^{d+1}\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{K}}^{P},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}=0
;

•

The graded pieces for
i=0,\dots,d
satisfy

\operatorname{Fil}^{i}/\operatorname{Fil}^{i+1}=\bigoplus_{g\in P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{S-\{p\}})/\widetilde{K}^{p}_{S}}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in{{}^{M}W}\\
l(w)=i\end{subarray}}\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}

where
x=w_{0}^{M}ww_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
acts on the summands for
w
through
\mathcal{S}_{w}
.

Proof.

First we work over
\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}
. Let
\mathcal{C}=R\Gamma(X_{M,K^{p}},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\mathrm{sm}}
(i.e., we no longer localise at
\mathfrak{m}
). We have Hecke-equivariant quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X^{P}_{\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda}}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma(T(b),\mathcal{O}(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda})\otimes R\Gamma(X^{P}_{\widetilde{K}^{p}},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{g\in P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{S-\{p\}})/\widetilde{K}^{p}_{S}}R\Gamma\Big(T(b),\mathcal{O}(w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda})\otimes I_{\geqslant 0}(\mathcal{C}))^{\operatorname{ord}}\Big);

the first is directly analogous to Lemma
9.5.2
, whilst the second is Proposition
9.3.5
.
This inherits the desired filtration (modulo
\varpi^{m}
) via (
9.5.4
), using Lemma
9.5.6
to describe the graded pieces.

To get the claimed filtration in characteristic zero, we pass to the limit over
m
, noting that all of the relevant inverse systems satisfy the Mittag-Leffler property (as they are inverse systems of finite
\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}
-modules).
∎

9.6.
Local-global compatibility

We now discuss the local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
.

9.6.1.
Characteristic zero local-global compatibility

Recall that
p>2n
and
\mathfrak{m}
is assumed to be decomposed generic and non-Eisenstein, and we assume that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
is decomposed generic. Let
c\geqslant b\geqslant 0
with
c\geqslant 1
, and let
\widetilde{K}
and
K
be as in §
9.3.3
with the additional condition that
\widetilde{K}_{p}=\widetilde{\operatorname{Iw}}(b,c)
and
K_{p}=\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)
.

Proposition 9.6.1
.

Let
\widetilde{\lambda}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{n};\widetilde{\lambda}_{0})\in X^{*}(T)^{+}
be a sufficiently regular weight, in the sense that
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{i+1}\geqslant n
for all
i=1,\dots,n-1
, and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{n}\geqslant n
. Then:

(1)

\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}}
is
\varpi
-torsion free, and
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}}[1/p]
is a semisimple module for the Hecke algebra
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
.

(2)

The Hecke algebra

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\big(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}}[1/p]\big)

is a finite product of characteristic zero fields. Each maximal ideal
\mathfrak{n}
corresponds to a Hecke eigensystem appearing in
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},L})
which is ordinary at
p
. Furthermore, there exists a continuous semisimple Galois representation
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n+1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
, such that for all
\ell\not\in S
,
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}
is unramified at
\ell
and

\operatorname{det}(1-\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}X|\rho_{\mathfrak{n}})

equals the image of
\widetilde{H}_{\ell}(X)
in
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}}[1/p])_{\mathfrak{n}}[X]
. Moreover, the residual representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{n}}
modulo
p
is isomorphic to
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
.

(3)

One has local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
, namely

\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},1}&*&*&*\\
&\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},2}&*&*\\
&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},2n+1}\end{array}\right)

where
\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},i}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}^{\times}
is the character as in Definition
9.1.2
associated with the algebra homomorphism
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with kernel
\mathfrak{n}
.

Proof.

Recall that we are assuming
n\geqslant 2
. The torsion subgroup
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}[\varpi]
is a quotient of
\operatorname{H}^{d-1}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda}}/\varpi)_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
, which is zero by Theorem
9.3.2
. Fix an isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\cong\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Via this isomorphism, we can consider the singular cohomology

\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathbb{C}})\cong\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}).

This cohomology group can be computed in terms of automorphic representations following
[
FS98
, §2.2]
. In particular, it has a Hecke-equivariant direct summand given by the cuspidal cohomology
\operatorname{H}^{d}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})
. As
\widetilde{\lambda}
is sufficiently regular, by
[
Sch94
, Corollary 2.3]
we have
\operatorname{H}^{d}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})=\operatorname{H}^{d}_{!}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})
; explicitly,
\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\operatorname{cusp}}
is the image of
\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{c}}
in the excision exact sequence

(9.6.2)

\mathrm{H}^{d}_{\mathrm{c}}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\to\mathrm{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\to\mathrm{H}^{d}(\partial\overline{X}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})\to\mathrm{H}^{d+1}_{\mathrm{c}}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}).

Now let
\mathfrak{n}
be a maximal ideal as in the statement. Base-changing to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, we obtain a maximal ideal
\mathfrak{b}\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
lying above
\mathfrak{n}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}
. Theorem
9.3.2
implies
\operatorname{H}^{d+1}_{c}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}=0,
so the same is true after inverting
p
and localising at
\mathfrak{b}
. Thus (
9.6.2
) induces a Hecke-equivariant isomorphism

(9.6.3)

\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}\cong\operatorname{H}^{d}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}\oplus\operatorname{H}^{d}(\partial\overline{X}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}

(and similarly after taking ordinary parts). We see that if
\mathfrak{b}
is a maximal ideal in
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}),
then it contributes to at least one of cuspidal or boundary cohomology. Suppose first it contributes to the cuspidal part; then by the usual description of cuspidal cohomology,
\mathfrak{b}
is the prime-to-
S
Hecke eigensystem associated with a regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A})
of weight
-w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\widetilde{\lambda}
which is ordinary at
p
, and we may take
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}=\rho_{\sigma}
via Theorem
9.2.4
.

To prove the rest of (1) and (2), we use a similar result for boundary cohomology; namely, we’ll show that if
\mathfrak{b}
contributes to boundary cohomology, then it arises as the pullback under
\mathcal{S}
of the Hecke eigensystem of a (uniquely determined) regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
M(\mathbb{A})
.

Tensoring Proposition
9.3.4
by
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and localising at
\mathfrak{b}
yields

\operatorname{H}^{d}(\partial\overline{X}_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\cong\operatorname{H}^{d}(X^{P}_{\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\operatorname{ord}}.

This latter space has a filtration induced from that in Proposition
9.5.9
, whose graded pieces are direct sums over
{{}^{M}}W
. Suppose that
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X^{P}_{\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0
; then some graded piece is non-zero, and hence there exists some
w\in{{}^{M}W}
and an ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
such that

\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathfrak{b}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0

(recalling
x=w_{0}^{M}ww_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}
). We now show, over two claims, that there is at most one such
w
, and that
\mathfrak{a}
is associated with a cuspidal automorphic representation of
M(\mathbb{A})
.

Claim 9.6.4
.

Suppose
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X^{P}_{\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0
, and let
\mathfrak{a}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
be as above. Then
\mathfrak{a}
is the prime-to-
S
Hecke eigensystem associated with a regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
M(\mathbb{A})
of weight
-w_{0}^{M}\lambda_{x}
which is ordinary at
p
.

Proof of claim:
The statements of Theorem
9.2.7
also hold in characteristic zero (by the same arguments as in the proof of
[
Sch15
, Corollary 5.4.2]
). This implies there exist semisimple Galois representations
\rho_{\mathfrak{b}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n+1}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
and
\rho_{\mathfrak{a}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
associated with
\mathfrak{b}
and
\mathfrak{a}
respectively satisfying local-global compatibility for
\ell\not\in S
. As
\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathfrak{b}
, by (
9.1.5
) these compatibilities imply

\rho_{\mathfrak{b}}\cong\rho_{\mathfrak{a}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus\rho_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\vee}(1).

Since
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{b}}\cong\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}\cong\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee}(1)
, we see that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{a}}
is isomorphic to either
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
or
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee}(2)
; thus
\rho_{\mathfrak{a}}
(and its residual representation) is absolutely irreducible. This implies that

\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\cong\operatorname{H}_{!}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\cong\operatorname{H}_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{a}}.

Indeed,
\mathfrak{a}
cannot contribute to the non-cuspidal part of the cohomology of
X_{M,K}
via the decomposition in
[
FS98
]
. If it did, then as explained in the proof of
[
ACC+23
, Theorem 2.4.10]
,
\mathfrak{a}
would correspond to a Hecke eigensystem appearing in an induction of a cuspidal automorphic representation of a proper Levi subgroup of
M
, and its associated Galois representation
\rho_{\mathfrak{a}}
would therefore be reducible, a contradiction. Thus
\mathfrak{a}
contributes only to interior cohomology, giving the first isomorphism. The second isomorphism follows from
[
Sch94
, Corollary 2.3]
and the fact that
\lambda_{x}
is regular (because
\widetilde{\lambda}
is sufficiently regular).

Claim
9.6.4
now follows from the usual description of cuspidal cohomology.

We note that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{a}}=\overline{\rho}_{\sigma}
is non-Eisenstein and decomposed generic (because it is isomorphic to either
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
or
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}^{\vee}(2)
), hence
\rho_{\sigma}
satisfies local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
(Theorem
9.2.6
).

Claim 9.6.5
.

There is at most one
w\in{{}^{M}}W
such that
\mathrm{H}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{b}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0
.

Proof of claim:
Suppose we have two distinct
w
and
w^{\prime}
(with associated
x,x^{\prime}
) with this non-vanishing property. Then as above, there exist
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}}
such that
\mathfrak{b}=\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathfrak{a}^{\prime})
and

\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{a}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0\text{ and }\operatorname{H}^{l(w^{\prime})}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x^{\prime}},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})_{\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}}^{\operatorname{ord}}\neq 0.

By Claim
9.6.4
,
\mathfrak{a}
and
\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}
correspond to cuspidal automorphic representations
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
of
M(\mathbb{A})
. By strong multiplicity one for cuspidal automorphic representations of
\GL_{n}
, we either have
\sigma^{\prime}\cong\sigma
or
\sigma^{\prime}\cong\sigma^{\vee}(2)
(the dual of
\sigma
twisted by
|\!|-|\!|^{2}
). The former cannot happen because
\lambda_{x}\neq\lambda_{x^{\prime}}
; so the latter holds. This implies that in
X^{*}(T_{\GL_{n}})
we have

\displaystyle\lambda_{x^{\prime}}=-w_{0}^{\GL_{n}}\lambda_{x}+(-2,\dots,-2)=-ww_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\cdot(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)+\big(w_{0}^{\GL_{n}}\cdot\rho\big)+(-2,\dots,-2).

Here the actions are computed in
X^{*}(T)
, but equality only holds after we project under
X^{*}(T)\to X^{*}(T_{\GL_{n}})
(omitting the last coordinate). In
X^{*}(T_{\GL_{n}})
we have
-w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}\cdot\widetilde{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}
, so

(9.6.6)

\displaystyle\lambda_{x^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=w\cdot(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)+\big(w_{0}^{\GL_{n}}\cdot\rho\big)+(-2,\dots,-2)

\displaystyle=\Big(w\cdot(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)-\rho\Big)+\Big(\rho+\big(w_{0}^{\GL_{n}}\cdot\rho\big)+(-2,\dots,-2)\Big)=\lambda_{w}+(n-1,\dots,n-1),

as
\rho=(n,\dots,1)
. In particular, since
n\geqslant 2
, we see
\lambda_{x^{\prime}}\neq\lambda_{w}
, hence
x^{\prime}\neq w
.

Write
\widetilde{\lambda}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\lambda}_{n};\widetilde{\lambda}_{0})
with
\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}>\widetilde{\lambda}_{2}>\cdots>\widetilde{\lambda}_{n}\geqslant n
sufficiently regular (recalling
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{i+1}\geqslant n
). Suppose that
w=w_{\mathcal{B}}
and
x^{\prime}=w_{\mathcal{C}}
for
\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C}\subset\{1,\dots,n\}
, with notation as in Lemma
9.1.1
. Explicitly, we have

(\lambda_{w_{\mathcal{B}}})_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{b_{i}^{\prime}}-\rho_{i}&\text{ if }1\leqslant i\leqslant\#\mathcal{B}^{c}\\
-(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{b_{n+1-i}}-\rho_{i}&\text{ if }\#\mathcal{B}^{c}+1\leqslant i\leqslant n\end{array}\right.,

and similarly for
\lambda_{w_{\mathcal{C}}}
. As
w\neq x^{\prime}
we have
\mathcal{B}\neq\mathcal{C}
, so
\mathcal{B}^{c}\neq\mathcal{C}^{c}
. We consider three cases:

•

If
\#\mathcal{C}^{c}>\#\mathcal{B}^{c}
, let
i=\#\mathcal{B}^{c}+1
. Then

(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{c_{i}^{\prime}}=-(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{b_{n+1-i}}+(n-1)\iff\widetilde{\lambda}_{c_{i}^{\prime}}+\widetilde{\lambda}_{b_{n+1-i}}=c_{i}^{\prime}+b_{n+1-i}-n-3,

which can’t happen as
\widetilde{\lambda}
is sufficiently regular; the left-hand side is
\geqslant 2n
, whilst the right-hand side is
\leqslant n-3
.

•

If
\#\mathcal{B}^{c}>\#\mathcal{C}^{c}
, let
i=\#\mathcal{C}^{c}+1
. Then

-(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{c_{n+1-i}}=(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{b_{i}^{\prime}}+(n-1),

impossible as the left-hand side is
<0
whereas the right-hand side is
>0
.

•

If
\#\mathcal{B}^{c}=\#\mathcal{C}^{c}
, let
1\leqslant i\leqslant\#\mathcal{B}^{c}
such that
b_{i}^{\prime}\neq c_{i}^{\prime}
. Then
(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{c_{i}^{\prime}}=(\widetilde{\lambda}+\rho)_{b^{\prime}_{i}}+(n-1)\iff

(9.6.7)

\widetilde{\lambda}_{c_{i}^{\prime}}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{b^{\prime}_{i}}=c_{i}^{\prime}-b_{i}^{\prime}+n-1.

As
\widetilde{\lambda}
is sufficiently regular, we have
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}|\geqslant|r-s|n
for all
r,s\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. The equality (
9.6.7
) implies that

|c_{i}^{\prime}-b_{i}^{\prime}|n\leqslant|\widetilde{\lambda}_{c_{i}^{\prime}}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{b_{i}^{\prime}}|=|c_{i}^{\prime}-b_{i}^{\prime}+n-1|\leqslant|c_{i}^{\prime}-b_{i}^{\prime}|+n-1,

hence
|c_{i}^{\prime}-b_{i}^{\prime}|=1
and
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{c_{i}^{\prime}}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{b_{i}^{\prime}}|=n
. This is incompatible with (
9.6.7
), noting that by regularity
\widetilde{\lambda}_{r}-\widetilde{\lambda}_{s}
and
r-s
have different signs if
r\neq s
.

Thus
\lambda_{x^{\prime}}\neq\lambda_{w}+(n-1,...,n-1)
, whence
\sigma^{\prime}\not\cong\sigma^{\vee}(2)
. By contradiction, Claim
9.6.5
holds.

We now complete the proof of Proposition
9.6.1
. Combining (
9.6.3
), the filtration from Proposition
9.5.9
, and both claims, we have

(9.6.8)

\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{b}}=\operatorname{H}_{\operatorname{cusp}}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{b}}\oplus\bigoplus_{g\in P(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{S-\{p\}})/\widetilde{K}^{p}_{S}}\operatorname{H}^{l(w)}_{\operatorname{cusp}}(X_{M,K},V_{\lambda_{x},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{a}}

for some
w\in{{}^{M}W}
and
\mathfrak{a}
as above (where the second summand is zero if no such
w
exists). Thus
\mathfrak{b}
arises from a regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A})
(if it contributes to the first summand) or
M(\mathbb{A})
(the second). As the ordinary part of
\sigma
is at most 1-dimensional, we see that
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
acts semisimply on
\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{b}}
, completing the proof of (1).

Finally, let
\mathfrak{n}
be as in the statement of the proposition. Base-changing to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
and using the above, we see
\mathfrak{n}
arises from some
p
-ordinary regular algebraic cuspidal automorphic representation
\sigma
of
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A})
or
M(\mathbb{A})
via the above. The first two parts of (2) follow. If
\mathfrak{n}
contributes to the cuspidal cohomology, as above we let
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}=\rho_{\sigma}
and conclude (2) and (3) by Theorem
9.2.4
. If
\mathfrak{n}
contributes to the boundary cohomology, then we set

\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}=\rho_{\sigma}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus\rho_{\sigma}^{\vee}(1).

By construction this satisfies the properties desired in (2). It remains to deduce (3) in this case. As explained above, the Galois representation
\rho_{\sigma}
satisfies local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
by Theorem
9.2.6
. Since
w\in{{}^{M}W}
, we may re-order the filtration on
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
so that all the desired properties are satisfied. More precisely, the parameters

\{\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},n+2},\dots,\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},2n+1},\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},1},\dots,\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda},n}\}

for
\rho_{\mathfrak{n}}
(excluding the trivial parameter) are in order

\{\zeta_{w(1)},\zeta_{w(2)},\dots,\zeta_{w(n)},\zeta_{w(n+1)},\dots,\zeta_{w(2n)}\}

where

\{\zeta_{1},\dots,\zeta_{2n}\}=\{\chi_{\lambda_{x},1}\otimes\omega^{-1},\dots,\chi_{\lambda_{x},n}\otimes\omega^{-1},\chi_{\lambda_{x},n}^{-1}\otimes\omega,\dots,\chi_{\lambda_{x},1}^{-1}\otimes\omega\}

in order. Here
\chi_{\lambda_{x},i}
are the characters associated with
\sigma
as in Definition
9.1.2
.
∎

We note the following important consequence of this proposition:

Corollary 9.6.9
.

Consider notation and assumptions as in Proposition
9.6.1
. There exists a continuous
(2n+1)
-dimensional determinant

\widetilde{D}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}})

such that for any
\mathfrak{n}
as in Proposition
9.6.1
(2), we have
\Theta_{\mathfrak{n}}\circ\widetilde{D}=\operatorname{det}(\rho_{\mathfrak{n}})
as maps
G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Here
\Theta_{\mathfrak{n}}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}})\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
is reduction modulo
\mathfrak{n}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
Che14
, Example 2.32]
applied to the embedding

\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}})\hookrightarrow\prod_{\mathfrak{n}}\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d}(X_{\widetilde{G},\widetilde{K}},V_{\widetilde{\lambda},\mathcal{O}})_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}^{\operatorname{ord}}[1/p])_{\mathfrak{n}}.\qed

9.6.2.
Some important lemmas

We pause to give two important technical lemmas.

For a weight
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})\in X^{*}(T_{\GL_{n}})
and integer
a\in\mathbb{Z}
, let
\lambda(a)\in X^{*}(T)
denote the weight

\lambda(a)=(\lambda(a)_{1},\dots,\lambda(a)_{n};0)=(\lambda_{1}+a,\dots,\lambda_{n}+a;0).

Lemma 9.6.10

(c.f.,
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 5.4.8]
)
.

Let
m\geqslant 1
be an integer. Then there exists a
\GL_{n}
-dominant weight
\lambda\in X^{*}(T_{\GL_{n}})
such that:

(1)

\mathcal{O}(\lambda)/\varpi^{m}\cong\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}
;

(2)

For all
0\leqslant j\leqslant d
, there exists
a_{j}\in(p-1)\mathbb{Z}
and
w_{j}\in{{}^{M}W}
with
l(w_{j})=d-j
, such that

\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=x_{j}^{-1}\star\lambda(a_{j})=x_{j}^{-1}\cdot(\lambda(a_{j})+\rho)-\rho

is
\widetilde{G}
-dominant. Here,
x_{j}=w_{0}^{M}w_{j}w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}
and
\rho
denotes the half-sum of the positive roots of
\widetilde{G}
.

(3)

The weight
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
in (2) is sufficiently regular as in Proposition
9.6.1
.

Proof.

Recall the explicit description of
{{}^{M}W}
from Lemma
9.1.1
, and note that
w_{0}^{M}w_{\mathcal{B}}w_{0}^{\widetilde{G}}=w_{{\mathcal{B}}^{c}}
. Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant d
, and let

{\mathcal{B}}_{j}=\{1,2,\dots,y-1,y,z\}

where
0\leqslant y<z\leqslant n+1
are integers such that
j=(y+1)(n+1)-(y+1)y/2-z
(if
y=0
, then
{\mathcal{B}}_{j}=\{z\}
, and if
z=n+1
, then
{\mathcal{B}}_{j}=\{1,\dots,y\}
). We set
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=\varnothing
. Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
be a
\GL_{n}
-dominant character, and let
a\in\mathbb{Z}
be an integer. Set
x_{j}=w_{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}}
and
w_{j}=w_{{\mathcal{B}}_{j}^{c}}
.

Let
C\gg n
be a sufficiently large integer (depending only on
n
) such that the absolute value of any entry of
w^{-1}\cdot\rho-\rho
(written as
(v_{1},\dots,v_{n};0)
, say) for any
w\in{{}^{M}W}
is
<C
. Let
M>8(C+n)
be a non-negative integer divisible by
8n(p-1)\#(\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\times}
. We claim that the weight

\lambda=((2^{n}-2)M,(2^{n}-4)M,(2^{n}-8)M,\dots,0)

works (i.e.,
\lambda=(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})
with
\lambda_{i}=(2^{n}-2^{i})M
).

We first calculate
x_{j}^{-1}\cdot\lambda(a)
using the description in Lemma
9.1.1
. We have:

\displaystyle x_{j}^{-1}\cdot\lambda(a)

\displaystyle=(\lambda(a)_{x_{j}(1)},\dots,\lambda(a)_{x_{j}(n)};*)

\displaystyle=(-\lambda(a)_{n},\dots,-\lambda(a)_{n+1-y},\lambda(a)_{1},\dots,\lambda(a)_{z-y-1},-\lambda(a)_{n-y},\lambda(a)_{z-y},\dots,\lambda(a)_{n-y-1};*).

If
j=0
(resp.
j=d
), then this is
\widetilde{G}
-dominant if only if
a\geqslant 0
(resp.
a\leqslant-(2^{n}-2)M
). Clearly, in both cases, we can find
a_{j}\in(p-1)\mathbb{Z}
such that
x_{j}^{-1}\star\lambda(a_{j})
is dominant and sufficiently regular (because
2(M-C)\geqslant n
). We therefore assume that
j\neq 0,d
.

The weight
x_{j}^{-1}\cdot\lambda(a)
is
\widetilde{G}
-dominant if and only if the following four inequalities are satisfied:
13
13

13

Here, in any of these inequalities, if the argument on the left-hand side (resp. right-hand side) is undefined, we replace it with
+\infty
(resp.
0
).

\displaystyle-(2^{n}-2^{n+1-y})M-a

\displaystyle\geqslant(2^{n}-2)M+a,

\displaystyle(2^{n}-2^{z-y-1})M+a

\displaystyle\geqslant-(2^{n}-2^{n-y})M-a,

\displaystyle-(2^{n}-2^{g-y})M-a

\displaystyle\geqslant(2^{n}-2^{z-y})M+a,

\displaystyle(2^{n}-2^{g-y-1})M+a

\displaystyle\geqslant 0.

If
y=0
(so
z\leqslant n
by our above assumption), then we see that this is implied if

-(2^{n}-2^{z-1})\tfrac{M}{2}\leqslant a\leqslant-(2^{n}-2^{z})\tfrac{M}{2}.

Note that this range has length
\geqslant M/2
. In particular, we can take
a
to be an integer in this range congruent to
M/8
modulo
M/2
, and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
is dominant and sufficiently regular (because one must have
-(2^{n}-2^{z-1})\tfrac{M}{2}+C+g\leqslant a\leqslant-(2^{n}-2^{z})\tfrac{M}{2}-C-n
by the condition
M>8(C+n)
).

Finally, we assume that
1\leqslant y\leqslant n-1
and
z>y+1
. Then the four inequalities are implied by

\displaystyle-(2^{n+1}-2^{n-y}-2^{z-y-1})\tfrac{M}{2}\leqslant a\leqslant-(2^{n+1}-2^{n-y}-2^{z-y})\tfrac{M}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\text{ if }z\neq n+1

\displaystyle-(2^{n+1}-2^{n+1-y})\tfrac{M}{2}\leqslant a\leqslant-(2^{n+1}-2^{n+1-y}-2)\tfrac{M}{2}

\displaystyle\quad\text{ if }z=n+1

and these ranges again have size
\geqslant M/2
. We can then take
a_{j}
in this range congruent to
M/8
modulo
M/2
, and for the same reasons above,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{j}
will be dominant and sufficiently regular.
∎

Lemma 9.6.11
.

Let
p
be an odd prime. Let
S_{0}
be a finite set of primes containing
p
and let
\ell\not\in S_{0}
. Then, for any finite set
T\subset\overline{\mathbb{F}}^{\times}_{p}
, there exists a finite set
S
of primes containing
S_{0}
but not containing
\ell
, and a finite-order character
\psi\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
such that

•

\psi
is unramified outside
S\backslash S_{0}
, and

•

\psi(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}),\psi(\operatorname{Frob}_{p})\not\in T
.

Proof.

Let
M\geqslant 1
be the largest integer such that
T
contains an
M
-th root of unity in
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
(note that
M
always exists). Let
q
be an odd prime with the following properties:

•

q
is coprime to
\ell p
,

•

q\not\equiv 1
modulo
p
, and

•

\ell^{M}<q
and
p^{M}<q
.

Such a prime exists by the Chebotarev density theorem applied to
\mathbb{Q}(\mu_{p})
. Let
a
denote the order of
\ell\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
and
b
the order of
p\in\left(\mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}\right)^{\times}
. Note that
a,b
are both prime to
p
by the second bullet point, and
a,b>M
by the third bullet point.

Let
S=S_{0}\cup\{q\}
and let
\chi
be a primitive Dirichlet character modulo
q
which maps a fixed choice of primitive root modulo
q
to a fixed choice of
(q-1)
-th root of unity
\zeta_{q-1}\in\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
. Then
\chi(\ell)
is a primitive
a
-th root of unity and
\chi(p)
is a primitive
b
-th root of unity. Let
\psi=\bar{\chi}
denote the reduction of
\chi
modulo
p
(viewed as a Galois character in the usual way). Then as
a,b
are prime to
p
, both
\psi(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})
and
\psi(\operatorname{Frob}_{p})
are roots of unity in
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}^{\times}
of order
>M
, so not contained in
T
.
∎

9.6.3.
Mixed characteristic local-global compatibility

We now discuss the mixed-characteristic local-global compatibility. Let
S
be a finite set of primes containing
p
, and let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\subset\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{S}
denote a maximal ideal with residue field
k=\mathcal{O}/\varpi
. We suppose that there exists a continuous semisimple Galois representation

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(k)

associated with
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
as in Assumption
9.3.1
. If
\psi\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
is a continuous finite-order character which is unramified outside
S
, then we let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)\subset\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{S}
denote the twist of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
by
\psi
. Explicitly, if
\Theta\colon\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}\to k
is the
\mathcal{O}
-algebra homomorphism with kernel
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
, then
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
is the kernel of

[\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{S})\cdot m\cdot\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{S})]\mapsto\overline{\psi}(\operatorname{det}m)\cdot\Theta([\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{S})\cdot m\cdot\operatorname{GL}_{n}(\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}^{S})]),\quad\quad m\in\operatorname{GL}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S}),

where
\overline{\psi}
denotes the mod
\varpi
reduction of
\psi
. Note that one has a Galois representation associated with
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
as in Assumption
9.3.1
, namely
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)}=\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\otimes\overline{\psi}
.

Proposition 9.6.12

(c.f.,
[
ACC+23
, Proposition 5.4.18]
)
.

Let
p>2n
. Suppose that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic. Let
c\geqslant b\geqslant 0
with
c\geqslant 1
, and let
K\subset\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denote a neat compact open subgroup which is hyperspecial outside
S
and equal to
\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)
at
p
. Let
m\geqslant 1
and let
\lambda
,
\{a_{j}\}
and
\{w_{j}\}
be as in Lemma
9.6.10
. Let
1\leqslant j\leqslant d
, and set

\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)=\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\right).

Then there exists a nilpotent ideal
J\subset\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)
(with nilpotence degree only depending on
n
), and a continuous semisimple representation

\varrho_{j}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{n}(\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)/J)

such that:

(1)

For every
\ell\notin S
,
\varrho_{j}
is unramified at
\ell
and the characteristic polynomial

\operatorname{det}(1-\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}X|\varrho_{j})

equals the image of the Hecke polynomial
H_{\ell}(X)
in
(\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)/J)[X]
.

(2)

For every
x\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, the characteristic polynomial of
\varrho_{j}(x)
equals
\prod_{i=1}^{n}(X-\chi_{\lambda,i}(x))
. Here we are viewing the characters
\chi_{\lambda,i}
from Definition
9.1.2
as being valued in
(\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)/J)^{\times}
.

(3)

For each
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, we have

(\varrho_{j}(x_{1})-\chi_{\lambda,1}(x_{1}))\cdots(\varrho_{j}(x_{n})-\chi_{\lambda,n}(x_{n}))=0.

Proof.

We first note that a Galois representation
\varrho_{j}
satisfying (1) already exists from the work of Scholze
[
Sch15
]
. We therefore want to verify (2) and (3) for this representation. Since this only concerns the behaviour at
p
, we are free to enlarge
S
, increase
\mathcal{O}
and deepen the level
K
at primes in
S-\{p\}
as we wish. Here we are using the fact that Galois representations over the Henselian semi-local
\mathcal{O}
-algebra
\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\lambda;j)
, reducing to
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
modulo each of its maximal ideals, are determined (up to isomorphism) by their associated determinant (see
[
Che14
, Theorem 2.22]
).

The first step is to set things up so that our running assumptions in the previous sections are satisfied. Suppose that
\ell\not\in S
is an odd prime where
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
is decomposed generic. After possibly enlarging
\mathcal{O}
and
S
, we can find a finite-order character
\psi\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
, unramified outside
S-\{p\}
, such that

(9.6.13)

(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\otimes\overline{\psi}\otimes\omega^{-1})\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}^{\vee}\otimes\overline{\psi}^{-1}\otimes\omega)

is decomposed generic at
\ell
(because
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
is). Moreover, we may assume that
(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\otimes\overline{\psi}\otimes\omega^{-1})|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
,
\mathbf{1}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
, and
(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}^{\vee}\otimes\overline{\psi}^{-1}\otimes\omega)|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
have no Jordan–Hölder factors in common with each other. Indeed, the existence of
\psi
follows from Lemma
9.6.11
for the set

T=\{\beta_{i}^{-1},\beta_{i}^{-1}\ell^{2},\pm\beta_{i}^{-1}\ell^{1/2},\pm\beta_{i}^{-1}\ell^{3/2},\pm\alpha_{i}^{-1}\}

where
\beta_{1},\dots,\beta_{n}
are the eigenvalues of
\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}
on
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
, and
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{n}
are the eigenvalues of a fixed choice of lift of Frobenius
\operatorname{Frob}_{p}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
at
p
on
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
. Note that
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
satisfies the same assumptions as
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
after possibly deepening the level
K
at primes in
S-\{p\}
. We will prove the result for this twist of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
, then untwist to get the result we actually want.

After enlarging
\mathcal{O}
and increasing
S
, we may assume that there exists a character
\chi\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}^{\times}
which is unramified outside
S-\{p\}
, such that the representations
[(\ref{Eqn:TwistedDecomGen})\otimes\bar{\chi}]|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
and
[(\ref{Eqn:TwistedDecomGen})\otimes\bar{\chi}^{-1}]|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
have no common Jordan–Hölder factors, and

(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\otimes\overline{\psi}\bar{\chi}\otimes\omega^{-1})\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}^{\vee}\otimes\overline{\psi}^{-1}\bar{\chi}^{-1}\otimes\omega)

is decomposed generic at
\ell
. We may also assume that
\bar{\chi}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
is non-trivial and doesn’t appear in
(\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\otimes\overline{\psi}\otimes\omega^{-1})|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
. Indeed, the existence of
\chi
follows from Lemma
9.6.11
for the set

\displaystyle T=\{\pm 1,\pm\alpha_{i}^{\pm 1/2}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{p})^{\pm 1/2},

\displaystyle\pm\alpha_{i}^{\pm 1}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{p})^{\pm 1},\beta_{i}^{-1}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})^{-1},\beta_{i}^{-1}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})^{-1}\ell^{2},

\displaystyle\pm\beta_{i}^{-1}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})^{-1}\ell^{1/2},\pm\beta_{i}^{-1}\bar{\psi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell})^{-1}\ell^{3/2}\}

where
\{\alpha_{i}\}
and
\{\beta_{i}\}
are as above. We can deepen
K
to include the ramification of
\chi
.

Notation
.

Set
\mathfrak{M}_{0}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}
to be either
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
or
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi\chi)
. Here,
S
is a finite set independent of the choice, which contains
p
and all primes where
\psi
and
\chi
ramify. We let
K_{M}\subset M(\mathbb{A}_{f})=\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\times\operatorname{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a sufficiently small compact open subgroup, which decomposes as
K_{M}=K\times C
for some compact open subgroup
C\subset\operatorname{GL}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
. After possibly shrinking
K
at primes in
S-\{p\}
and choosing
C
appropriately, we may assume that there exists a sufficiently small compact open subgroup
\widetilde{K}\subset\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
such that

\widetilde{K}\cap P(\mathbb{A}_{f})=(\widetilde{K}\cap N(\mathbb{A}_{f}))K_{M}

and
\widetilde{K}_{p}=\widetilde{\operatorname{Iw}}(b,c)
. We can also assume that
\widetilde{K}
is hyperspecial outside
S
and
\widetilde{K}^{p}_{S}
consists of matrices in
\widetilde{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{S-\{p\}})
which are congruent to the identity modulo
N_{S}
, for some integer
N_{S}
divisible only by primes in
S
. In other words
\widetilde{K}
and
K
satisfy the conditions in §
9.3.3
. Finally,
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}}
is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic, and
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus\overline{\rho}^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}}(1)
is decomposed generic.

Let
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S}
denote the preimage of
\mathfrak{M}_{0}
under the natural map
\mathbf{T}^{S}\to\mathbf{T}^{S}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}
induced from the projection
M=\operatorname{GL}_{n}\times\operatorname{GL}_{1}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}
. Set
\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathcal{S}^{*}(\mathfrak{m})
and note that
\overline{\rho}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{m}}}
is isomorphic to
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus\overline{\rho}^{\vee}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}}(1)
.

Note that the (real) dimension of the locally symmetric space associated with
M
is
d-1=n(n+1)/2-1
. The Künneth formula provides us with an isomorphism

\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{M,K_{M}},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})\cong\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{1},C},\mathcal{O}).

But
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{1},C},\mathcal{O})
a finite free
\mathcal{O}
-module with rank equal to the number of connected components of
X_{\operatorname{GL}_{1},C}
, and contains the trivial representation
\mathcal{O}
of the Hecke algebra for
\operatorname{GL}_{1}
. This implies that we have a natural map

\operatorname{pr}_{j}\colon\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{M,K_{M}},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}})\twoheadrightarrow\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})

induced from mapping a Hecke operator for
M
to one for
\GL_{n}
(via the natural projection
M\twoheadrightarrow\GL_{n}
).

Let
\chi_{\operatorname{cycl}}(x)=|\!|x|\!|x_{p}
for
x\in\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\times}
denote the adelic
p
-adic cyclotomic character. By precomposing with the natural map
\GL_{n}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{det}^{-a_{j}}}\operatorname{GL}_{1}
, this character gives rise to a class in
\operatorname{H}^{0}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\mu(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})
, where
\mu(a_{j})=(a_{j},\dots,a_{j})
. By considering the cup product with this class, we obtain an isomorphism

\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})\cong\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})\{a_{j}\}

where the notation
\{a_{j}\}
means:
[K^{S}mK^{S}]
acts as
\chi_{\operatorname{cycl}}(\operatorname{det}(m))^{-a_{j}}[K^{S}mK^{S}]
, where
m\in\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})
, and for
u\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}^{\times}
, the operator
\langle u\rangle_{i}
acts via
u^{a_{j}}\langle u\rangle_{i}
. This isomorphism is equivariant for the action of
U_{t}
for
t\in T_{\GL_{n}}^{+}
(recall that the action of these Hecke operators is automatically optimally normalised). This implies that the ordinary parts match up, and since
(p-1)
divides
a_{j}
, one also obtains an isomorphism after localising both sides at
\mathfrak{M}_{0}
. We therefore obtain an isomorphism

\nu_{j}\colon\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})

which sends
[K^{S}mK^{S}]
to
\chi_{\operatorname{cycl}}(\operatorname{det}(m))^{-a_{j}}[K^{S}mK^{S}]
,
U_{t}
to itself, and
\langle u\rangle_{i}
to
u^{a_{j}}\langle u\rangle_{i}
.

Notation
.

Let

\varrho_{j}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{n}\left(\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})/J_{j}\right)

denote the semisimple Galois representation satisfying property (1) in the statement of the proposition, as constructed in
[
Sch15
]
. Set
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}=(\nu^{-1}_{j}\circ\varrho_{j})\otimes\omega^{-a_{j}}
, and
J_{j}^{\prime}=\nu^{-1}_{j}(J_{j})
; so the representation
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}
is valued in
\GL_{n}(\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})/J_{j}^{\prime})
. Let
\chi_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}^{\prime}=\chi_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}\cdot\omega^{-a_{j}}
valued in
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})^{\times}
.

We now apply Proposition
9.5.8
. More precisely, we obtain a
\mathcal{O}
-algebra morphism

\mathcal{S}_{w_{j}}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{M,K_{M}},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}})

induced from
\mathcal{S}_{w_{j}}
. For
i=1,\dots,2n+1
, let
\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})^{\times}
denote the character from Definition
9.1.2
.

Let

\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})

denote the composition
\operatorname{pr}_{j}\circ\;\mathcal{S}_{w_{j}}
. One has for
1\leqslant i\leqslant n
:

\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},n+1-i}^{-1}=\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},n+1+i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\chi_{\lambda(a_{j}),w_{j}(i)}^{\prime}\otimes\omega^{-1}&\text{ if }1\leqslant w_{j}(i)\leqslant n\\
(\chi_{\lambda(a_{j}),2n+1-w_{j}(i)}^{\prime})^{-1}\otimes\omega&\text{ if }n+1\leqslant w_{j}(i)\leqslant 2n\end{array}\right.

and
\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},n+1}
is the trivial character. Furthermore
\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\widetilde{H}_{\ell}(X)
modulo
J_{j}^{\prime}
is the (reverse) characteristic polynomial of
\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}
on
\varrho_{j}^{\prime}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\varrho^{\prime}_{j})^{\vee}(1)
.

As explained in Corollary
9.6.9
implies that there exists a continuous determinant

\widetilde{D}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})

which, after inverting
p
, is the determinant associated with a continuous semisimple Galois representation
\sigma\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{2n+1}(\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})[1/p])
which satisfies:

•

For all
\ell\notin S
, the (reverse) characteristic polynomial of
\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}
is the image of
\widetilde{H}_{\ell}(X)
.

•

One has

\sigma|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},1}&*&*&*\\
&\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},2}&*&*\\
&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},2n+1}\end{array}\right).

Let
D=\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\widetilde{D}
. Then
D
modulo
J_{j}^{\prime}
is the continuous determinant associated with
\varrho_{j}^{\prime}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\varrho^{\prime}_{j})^{\vee}(1)
. To ease notation, set

\displaystyle\widetilde{A}_{0}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c,\widetilde{\lambda}_{j})

\displaystyle A_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda(a_{j}),\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{M}_{0}})

which are both semi-local finite
\mathcal{O}
-algebras. Let
A
be a local direct factor of
A_{0}
and let
\widetilde{A}
be the corresponding local direct factor of
\widetilde{A}_{0}
. It suffices to work over these local algebras. Let
J
denote the ideal
J^{\prime}_{j}
in the local factor
A
. Since
D
modulo
J
is the continuous determinant associated with
\varrho_{j}^{\prime}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\varrho^{\prime}_{j})^{\vee}(1)
, we have

(9.6.14)

\left(\overline{\varrho}_{j}^{\prime}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus(\overline{\varrho}^{\prime}_{j}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})^{\vee}(1)\right)^{\operatorname{ss}}\cong\mathbf{1}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}\omega^{-1}\oplus(\overline{\chi}^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i})^{-1}\omega

where the overline denotes reduction modulo the maximal ideal of
A
.

From now on, any of the the above notation refers to the objects associated with
\mathfrak{M}_{0}=\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
only. Rewriting (
9.6.14
) for the case
\mathfrak{M}_{0}=\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi\chi)
, we obtain the decomposition:

\left((\overline{\varrho}_{j}^{\prime}\otimes\overline{\chi})|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}(-1)\oplus\mathbf{1}\oplus((\overline{\varrho}^{\prime}_{j}\otimes\overline{\chi})|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})^{\vee}(1)\right)^{\operatorname{ss}}\cong\mathbf{1}\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}\overline{\chi}\omega^{-1}\oplus(\overline{\chi}^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i})^{-1}\overline{\chi}^{-1}\omega.

Our conditions on
\chi
then imply that:

(9.6.15)

(\overline{\varrho}^{\prime}_{j}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}})^{\operatorname{ss}}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}\overline{\chi}^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}.

We now argue as in the proof of
[
ACC+23
, Proposition 4.4.6, Proposition 5.4.18]
. More precisely, let
D_{A/J}=D\otimes_{A}A/J
, then we can identify

(A/J)[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]/\operatorname{ker}(D_{A/J})=M_{n}(A/J)\times M_{1}(A/J)\times M_{n}(A/J)

where the projection to the first factor corresponds to
\varrho_{j}^{\prime}(-1)
, the second to
\mathbf{1}
, and the third to
(\varrho_{j}^{\prime})^{\vee}(1)
.

On the other hand, the natural map
\widetilde{A}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]\to(A/J)[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]/\operatorname{ker}(D_{A/J})
factors through
\widetilde{A}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]/\operatorname{ker}(\widetilde{D})
, and we have an algebra embedding

\widetilde{A}[G_{\mathbb{Q}}]/\operatorname{ker}(\widetilde{D})\subset M_{2n+1}(\widetilde{A}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})

corresponding to
\sigma
. From the local-global compatibility for
\sigma|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}
, we see that for elements
Y,Y_{1},\dots,Y_{2n+1}
in
(A/J)[G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]
, we have

(9.6.16)

\operatorname{det}(X-\varrho_{j}^{\prime}(-1)(Y))\cdot(X-\mathbf{1}(Y))\cdot\operatorname{det}(X-(\varrho_{j}^{\prime})^{\vee}(1)(Y))=\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}(X-\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}(Y))

in
(A/J)[X]
(note we may view
\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}
as valued in
A/J
), and

(9.6.17)

(\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}(\varrho^{\prime}_{j}(-1)(Y_{i})-\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}(Y_{i})),\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}(\mathbf{1}(Y)-\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}(Y_{i})),\prod_{i=1}^{2n+1}((\varrho^{\prime}_{j})^{\vee}(1)(Y_{i})-\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}(Y_{i})))=(0,0,0)

in
M_{n}(A/J)\times M_{1}(A/J)\times M_{n}(A/J)
. By choosing suitable idempotents as in the proof of
[
ACC+23
, Proposition 5.4.18]
, we can deduce the desired properties for
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}
. More precisely, we can find an idempotent
e\in(A/J)[G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}]
such that
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}(-1)(e)=1
and the image of
e
under the trivial representation and
(\varrho_{j}^{\prime})^{\vee}(1)
is zero. We can also assume that
\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}(e)=1
if
\overline{\psi}_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}
appears in
\overline{\varrho}^{\prime}_{j}(-1)
, and is zero otherwise.

Let
x,x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
. Taking
Y=xe
in the identity (
9.6.16
), we see that

X^{n+1}\operatorname{det}(X-\varrho^{\prime}_{j}(-1)(x))=X^{n+1}\prod_{i=1}^{n}(X-\chi^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}\omega^{-1}(x)).

Similarly, let
Y_{i}=x_{k}e
if
\mathcal{T}_{w_{j}}\circ\psi_{\widetilde{\lambda}_{j},i}=\chi^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),k}\otimes\omega^{-1}
, and
Y_{i}=e
otherwise. Then plugging this into (
9.6.17
), we find that

(\prod_{k=1}^{n}(\varrho^{\prime}_{j}(-1)(x_{k})-\chi^{\prime}_{\lambda(a_{j}),k}\omega^{-1}(x_{k})),0,0)=(0,0,0).

This proves the proposition for
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}(-1)
and hence
\varrho^{\prime}_{j}
. Noting that
\nu_{j}\circ\chi_{\lambda(a_{j}),i}=\chi_{\lambda,i}
, we now untwist to obtain the proposition for
\varrho_{j}
, i.e., for the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
.

Finally, we note that because
\psi
is unramified outside
S-\{p\}
, we have an isomorphism

\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\right)\cong\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\left(\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)}\right)

which identifies the Galois representation
\varrho_{j}
(associated with
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
) with the one associated with
\mathfrak{m}_{0}(\psi)
twisted by the character
\psi^{-1}
. This implies the required local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
for
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
.
∎

Remark 9.6.18
.

With notation as above, suppose that
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
is in the support of
\operatorname{H}^{i}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{n},K},k)^{\operatorname{ord}}
for some appropriately chosen
i
and
b,c
, and let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}^{\prime}\subset\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{i}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{n},K},k)^{\operatorname{ord}})
be any maximal ideal extending
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
. After possibly increasing
k
, assume that the residue field of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}^{\prime}
is
k
. Then, as part of the proof of Proposition
9.6.12
, we have also established local-global compatibility at
\ell=p
for the Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
(assuming it is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic). In particular, if the characters
\chi_{i}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}\to k^{\times}
(from Definition
9.1.2
associated with the morphism
\Theta^{\prime}\colon\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{n}}/\mathfrak{m}_{0}^{\prime}=k
) are distinct, then combining the above with
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 6.2.11]
shows

\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}\chi_{1}&*&*&*\\
&\chi_{2}&*&*\\
&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&\chi_{n}\end{array}\right).

9.7.
The main results

We now discuss the main result of this section.

Theorem 9.7.1
.

Let
p>2n
. Let
c\geqslant b\geqslant 0
with
c\geqslant 1
, and let
K\subset\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
denote a neat compact open subgroup which is hyperspecial outside
S
and equal to
\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)
at
p
. Let
m\geqslant 1
and let

\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c;m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma(X_{\GL_{n},K},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})\subset\operatorname{End}_{\mathbf{D}(\mathcal{O})}(R\Gamma(X_{\GL_{n},K},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}).

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\subset\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c;m)
denote a maximal ideal with residue field
k=\mathcal{O}/\varpi
, and suppose that the associated Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic.

Then there exists a nilpotent ideal
J\subset\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c;m)_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
with nilpotence degree only depending on
n
, and a continuous semisimple representation

\rho_{b,c,m}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{n}(\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c;m)_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J)

such that:

(1)

For every
\ell\not\in S
, one has

\operatorname{det}(1-\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}X|\rho_{b,c,m})=\vartheta(H_{\ell}(X))

where
\vartheta\colon\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n},\mathcal{O}}\to\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(b,c;m)_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J
is the natural map.

(2)

For every
x\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, the characteristic polynomial of
\rho_{b,c,m}(x)
equals
\prod_{i=1}^{n}(X-\chi_{i}(x))
, where
\chi_{i}
are the characters from Definition
9.1.2
associated with
\vartheta
.

(3)

For each
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, we have

(\rho_{b,c,m}(x_{1})-\chi_{1}(x_{1}))\cdots(\rho_{b,c,m}(x_{n})-\chi_{n}(x_{n}))=0.

Proof.

We first note that the Galois representation
\rho_{b,c,m}
satisfying (1) already exists from the work of Scholze
[
Sch15
]
and Newton–Thorne
[
NT16
]
(see also
[
CGH+20
]
). Fix
1\leqslant j\leqslant d
, and let
\lambda
be as in Lemma
9.6.10
. We have a short exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}/\varpi^{m}\to\operatorname{H}^{d-j}(X_{\GL_{n},K},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}\to\operatorname{H}^{d-j+1}(X_{\GL_{n},K},V_{\lambda,\mathcal{O}})^{\operatorname{ord}}[\varpi^{m}]\to 0

because
\mathcal{O}(\lambda)/\varpi^{m}\cong\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}
and the ordinary part mod
\varpi^{m}
only depends on the weight modulo
\varpi^{m}
. The result now follows from Proposition
9.6.12
(note that the kernel of the map

\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma(X_{\GL_{n},K},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})\to\mathbf{T}_{\GL_{n}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{*}(X_{\GL_{n},K},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})

is nilpotent, with nilpotence degree only depending on
n
).
∎

We note the following corollary, which is what we will use in practice. Note that, if we let
K(b,c)=K
to emphasise the dependence on
b,c
, then since
R\Gamma(T(b),R\Gamma(X_{\GL_{n},K(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})\cong R\Gamma(X_{\GL_{n},K(b,c)},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}
for
b^{\prime}\geqslant b
and
c^{\prime}\geqslant c
, we obtain a natural
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}
-algebra morphism
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b^{\prime},c^{\prime};m)\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b,c;m)
. Similarly, we have natural maps
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b,c;m^{\prime})\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b,c;m)
for
m^{\prime}\geqslant m
. Here it is important that we are working with Hecke algebras in the derived categories, otherwise these maps do not exist in general.

Let
\mathcal{T}=\varprojlim_{b,c,m}\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b,c;m)
which we equip with the inverse limit topology. The ring
\mathcal{T}
is naturally a finite
\Lambda
-algebra, where
\Lambda=\mathcal{O}[\![T_{\operatorname{GL_{n}}}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})]\!]
(equipped with the usual pro-finite topology), and the topology on
\mathcal{T}
coincides with the canonical topology induced from that on
\Lambda
. Note that any maximal ideal
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\subset\mathcal{T}
with finite residue field descends to a maximal ideal of some
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}(b,c;m)
.

Corollary 9.7.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\subset\mathcal{T}
be a maximal ideal with residue field
k=\mathcal{O}/\varpi
, and suppose that the associated Galois representation
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\operatorname{GL}_{n}(k)
is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic. Then there exists a nilpotent ideal
J\subset\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
(with nilpotence degree only depending on
n
) and a continuous semisimple representation

\rho\colon G_{\mathbb{Q}}\to\GL_{n}(\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J)

such that:

(1)

For every
\ell\notin S
, the representation
\rho
is unramified at
\ell
and
\operatorname{det}(1-\operatorname{Frob}_{\ell}^{-1}X|\rho)
equals the image of
H_{\ell}(X)
in
(\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J)[X]
.

(2)

For every
x\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, one has

\operatorname{det}(X-\rho(x))=\prod_{i=1}^{n}(X-\chi_{i}(x))

where
\chi_{i}
denote the characters of Definition
9.1.2
valued in
(\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J)^{\times}
.

(3)

For each
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, one has

(\rho(x_{1})-\chi_{1}(x_{1}))\cdots(\rho(x_{n})-\chi_{n}(x_{n}))=0.

Moreover, let
L[\epsilon]
denote the ring of dual numbers and suppose that
f\colon\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J\to L[\epsilon]
is a
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\GL_{n}}
-algebra morphism. Set
\rho_{L[\epsilon]}=f\circ\rho
. Suppose that the characters
f\circ\chi_{i}
are pairwise distinct modulo
\epsilon
. Then

\rho_{L[\epsilon]}|_{G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}}\sim\left(\begin{array}[]{cccc}f\circ\chi_{1}&*&*&*\\
&f\circ\chi_{2}&*&*\\
&&\ddots&\vdots\\
&&&f\circ\chi_{n}\end{array}\right).

Proof.

There exists a nilpotent ideal
J
and continuous determinant
D
valued in
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J
with properties (1)–(3) by
[
Che14
, Example 2.32]
and Theorem
9.7.1
(crucially using that the nilpotence degree of the ideal is independent of
b,c,m
). Note that
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J
is a local Henselian ring and this determinant reduces to
\operatorname{det}(\overline{\rho}_{\pi})
modulo the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}/J
. The determinant
D
is Cayley–Hamilton (as it is built from Cayley–Hamilton determinants). We now obtain
\rho
by
[
Che14
, Theorem 2.22(i)]
and the fact that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
is assumed to be absolutely irreducible.

For the last part, we follow
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 6.2.11]
. Namely, let
V
denote the finite free of
L[\epsilon]
-module (of rank
n
) on which
\rho_{L[\epsilon]}
acts. Define, iteratively,
W_{i}\subset V/W_{i-1}
to be the largest submodule for which
\rho_{L[\epsilon]}
acts through the character
f\circ\chi_{i}
(with
W_{0}=0
). Let
V_{i}\subset V
denote the preimage of
W_{i}
under the map
V\to V/V_{i-1}
. Note that by property (3), we have
V_{n}=V
. We need to show that
V_{i}
is free of rank
i
.

Consider
V/V_{n-1}
. By property (3), we already know that
G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
acts through
f\circ\chi_{n}
on
V/V_{n-1}
. Furthermore, by
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 2.6.11]
(applied to the field
L=L[\epsilon]/\epsilon
), we must have

\operatorname{dim}_{L}(V/V_{n-1})/\epsilon=1

(it cannot be zero, because this would imply
\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(x-f\circ\chi_{i}(x))
would kill
V
modulo
\epsilon
for all
x\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
). By Nakayama’s lemma, we see that
V/V_{n-1}\neq 0
and is free of rank one over
L[\epsilon]
if we show it is
\epsilon
-torsion free.

Suppose there exists
v\in V
which is non-zero in
V/V_{n-1}
and satisfies
\epsilon v\in V_{n-1}
. By assumption, we can choose
x_{i}\in G_{\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
such that
f\chi_{n}(x_{i})-f\chi_{i}(x_{i})\in L[\epsilon]
is invertible for all
i=1,\dots,n-1
. Then

0=\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(\rho_{L[\epsilon]}(x_{i})-f\chi_{i}(x_{i}))\epsilon v=\epsilon\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(f\chi_{n}(x_{i})-f\chi_{i}(x_{i}))v+\epsilon w

for some
w\in V_{n-1}
(because
\rho_{L[\epsilon]}(x_{i})-f\chi_{n}(x_{i})
kills
V/V_{n-1}
). Since
V
is
\epsilon
-torsion free, we see that

v=-\prod_{i=1}^{n-1}(f\chi_{n}(x_{i})-f\chi_{i}(x_{i}))^{-1}w\;\in\;V_{n-1}

which is a contradiction. Hence
V/V_{n-1}
is
\epsilon
-torsion free, and hence free of rank one over
L[\epsilon]
. This implies
V_{n-1}
is free of rank
n-1
over
L[\epsilon]
, and we conclude by an inductive argument.
∎

9.8.
Proof of Theorem
8.3.5

We now specialise to
n=3
and use notation from §
8.1
freely. In particular
\Sigma\subset\Omega\subset\mathcal{W}
are affinoids in weight space. We recall that
\pi
is a regular algebraic, cuspidal automorphic representation of
\operatorname{GL}_{3}(\mathbb{A})
which has trivial cohomological weight and is Steinberg at
p
. In particular,
\pi_{p}
is ordinary. Recall that Theorem
8.3.5
asks for a triangulation of the Galois representation in an infinitesimal neighbourhood of
\pi
in the eigenvariety constructed with overconvergent cohomology; whilst Corollary
9.7.2
gives such a triangulation for the eigenvariety constructed using completed cohomology. To prove Theorem
8.3.5
, we essentially need to compare these eigenvarieties (around ordinary points); we do this below. A general comparison around finite-slope points will appear in forthcoming work of Johansson, McDonald and Tarrach.

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}
denote the mod
\varpi
Hecke eigensystem associated with
\pi
, and note that
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
is in the support of
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}(0,1;m)
for some
m\geqslant 1
. We are assuming that
\overline{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}=\overline{\rho}_{\pi}
is absolutely irreducible and decomposed generic. Recall the characters
\underline{\alpha}^{S},\underline{\alpha}_{p}
from §
8.1
, describing families of eigenvalues appearing in overconvergent cohomology. We obtain an
\mathcal{O}
-algebra homomorphism

\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}\xrightarrow{\underline{\alpha}^{S}\otimes\underline{\delta}_{p}}\mathcal{O}(\Sigma)\to L[\epsilon].

Here the second map is induced from
T_{1}\mapsto v_{1}\epsilon
and
T_{2}\mapsto v_{2}\epsilon
, and
\underline{\delta}_{p}\colon T^{+}\to\mathcal{O}(\Sigma)^{\times}
denotes the character

\underline{\delta}_{p}(t)=\underline{\alpha}_{p}(t)\kappa_{\Sigma}([t])

with
\kappa_{\Sigma}
equal to the universal character of
\Sigma
and
[t]\in T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
denoting the image of
t
under the natural projection map
T(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\twoheadrightarrow T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. To prove Theorem
8.3.5
, it suffices to show that this map factors through the natural map
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}\to\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}^{\operatorname{red}}
(where
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}^{\operatorname{red}}
is the maximal reduced quotient of
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
). We can then apply Corollary
9.7.2
. Since
\mathcal{O}(\Sigma)
is reduced, it suffices to show that
\underline{\alpha}^{S}\otimes\underline{\delta}_{p}
factors through
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}\to\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}=(\varprojlim_{b,c,m}\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}(b,c;m))_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
.

At the expense of shrinking
\Omega
, we may assume that
\Omega\subset\Omega^{\circ}\subset\Omega_{0}
, where
\Omega_{0}
is a (sufficiently small) open affinoid neighbourhood of the trivial weight in
\mathcal{W}
and
\Omega^{\circ}
is a wide open disc, i.e.,
\Omega^{\circ}
is the adic generic fibre of
\operatorname{Spf}\mathcal{O}[\![T_{1}/p^{\rho},T_{2}/p^{\rho}]\!]
for some
\rho\in\mathbb{Q}_{>0}
. Let
D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
denote the
\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
-linear dual of
A_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
(the space of
r
-analytic (in the variables corresponding to
\operatorname{Iw}
) functions
f:\operatorname{Iw}\to\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
which satisfy
f(b\cdot-)=\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}(b)\cdot f(-)
for all
b\in\overline{B}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})\cap\operatorname{Iw}
). The module
D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
carries actions of
(\operatorname{Iw},T^{+})
via the same formulae as in §
8.1
.

The representation
D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
is profinite, and has a
(\operatorname{Iw},T^{+})
-stable filtration
\operatorname{Fil}^{i}D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
satisfying (see
[
Han17
, §2]
and
[
JN19a
, §5.1]
):

•

\operatorname{Fil}^{0}D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}=D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
and
\bigcap_{i}\operatorname{Fil}^{i}D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}=0
;

•

D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}=\varprojlim_{i}D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{i}
;

•

Each
D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{i}
is finite, and the action of
\operatorname{Iw}_{i+r}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{ker}(\operatorname{Iw}\to\operatorname{GL}_{3}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{i+r}\mathbb{Z}))
on this module is trivial.

Let
F_{\Omega^{\circ}}\colon B(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}}\Omega^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
denote the natural character, which we view as an element of
A_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}
in the natural way. We have an
\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
-linear map

\Lambda\colon D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}\to\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})(\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{-1})

given by evaluating at
F_{\Omega^{\circ}}
. This map is equivariant for the action of
B(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
as well as the action of
T^{+}
(where
T^{+}
acts via the projection
T^{+}\twoheadrightarrow T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
on the right-hand side). Let
\Lambda_{m}\colon\colon D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m}\to(\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})/\mathfrak{a}^{m})(\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{-1})
denote the induced map, where
\mathfrak{a}
denotes the maximal ideal of
\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
. We have the following lemma:

Lemma 9.8.1
.

Let
m\geqslant 1
and
c\geqslant b\geqslant m+r
. The map
\Lambda_{m}
induces a
\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}
-equivariant quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},

\displaystyle D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}\cong R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}(0,m+r)},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}(0,m+r)},(\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})/\mathfrak{a}^{m})(\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{-1}))^{\operatorname{ord}}

\displaystyle\cong R\Gamma(T(\mathbb{Z}_{p})/T(b),R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m}}(\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})/\mathfrak{a}^{m})(\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{-1})).

In particular, the map

\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}\to\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{\operatorname{S},\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})

which is the natural one away from
S
, and is given by
t\mapsto U_{t}\cdot\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}([t])
for
t\in T^{+}
, factors through

\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}(b,c;m)=\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)},\mathcal{O}/\varpi^{m})^{\operatorname{ord}})

compatibly with the change of
b,c,m
maps.

Proof.

The morphism
\Lambda_{m}
is equivariant for the action of
(\operatorname{Iw}(0,m+r),T^{+})
, so the map on cohomology complexes is well-defined. The first and third isomorphisms are standard using the fact that the ordinary part at level
\operatorname{Iw}(b,c)
is independent of
c
and
[
ACC+23
, Lemma 5.2.9]
. Finally, the second map is a quasi-isomorphism, because one can show that the action of
t=\operatorname{diag}(p^{2},p,1)\in T^{+}
satisfies:
t\cdot\operatorname{ker}\Lambda_{m}\subset p\operatorname{ker}\Lambda_{m}
(hence the ordinary part with coefficients in
\operatorname{ker}\Lambda_{m}
vanishes).
∎

Let
\mathfrak{n}_{0}\subset\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})}
denote the maximal ideal given by the preimage of
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
under the map
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})}\to\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},\mathcal{O}}
given by reduction modulo
(T_{1}/p^{\rho},T_{2}/\rho^{\rho})
. Since
\mathfrak{m}_{0}
is non-Eisenstein, we also have

\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{\operatorname{S},\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}})=\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{\operatorname{S},\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma_{c}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}}).

Passing to the limit, and using the fact that
R\Gamma_{c}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}}
is a perfect complex in the derived category of
\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})
-modules, we see that the map

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}

\displaystyle\to\varprojlim_{m}\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma_{c}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}}/\operatorname{Fil}^{m})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(R\Gamma_{c}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}})

\displaystyle\to\mathbf{T}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}(\operatorname{H}^{*}_{c}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}})_{\mathfrak{n}_{0}}^{\operatorname{ord}})

satisfying
t\mapsto U_{t}\cdot\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}([t])
for
t\in T^{+}
, factors through
\mathcal{T}_{\mathfrak{m}_{0}}
(note that
\kappa_{\Omega^{\circ}}
is trivial modulo
(T_{1}/p^{\rho},T_{2}/p^{\rho})
).

To conclude the proof of Theorem
8.3.5
, we now note that

\operatorname{H}_{c}^{*}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}})^{\operatorname{ord}}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}^{+}(\Omega^{\circ})}\mathcal{O}(\Omega)\cong\operatorname{H}_{c}^{*}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega}^{r\operatorname{-an}})^{\operatorname{ord}}

Hecke-equivariantly, and we have a natural map

(9.8.2)

\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}(\operatorname{H}_{c}^{*}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega}^{r\operatorname{-an}})^{\operatorname{ord}})\to\mathcal{O}(\Sigma)

given by identifying
\Sigma
as an irreducible component of
\mathscr{E}\times_{\mathcal{W}}\Omega
passing through the point
x_{\pi}
. Here
\mathscr{E}\to\mathcal{W}
is the
\operatorname{GL}(3)
-eigenvariety in
[
Han17
]
constructed from (compactly-supported) overconvergent cohomology, and
x_{\pi}\in\mathscr{E}
is the point corresponding to the unique
p
-stabillisation in
\pi
. By design, the map (
9.8.2
) factors
\underline{\alpha}^{S}\otimes\underline{\alpha}_{p}
, and the induced map
\mathbf{T}^{S,\operatorname{ord}}_{\operatorname{GL}_{3}}(\operatorname{H}_{c}^{*}(X_{\operatorname{GL}_{3},K^{p}\operatorname{Iw}},D_{\Omega^{\circ}}^{\circ,r\operatorname{-an}})^{\operatorname{ord}})\to\mathcal{O}(\Sigma)
factors through the localisation at
\mathfrak{n}_{0}
(because the specialisation of
\underline{\alpha}^{S}\otimes\underline{\alpha}_{p}
at the trivial weight is the Hecke eigensystem associated with
\pi
).

Remark 9.8.3
.

Note that, following this method, one can also obtain results about triangulations in families for ordinary points on the
\GL_{n}
-eigenvariety, for general
n\geqslant 2
. We leave the details to the interested reader.
